Summary of branch dsu_linux_porting_2 (revision 222 to 267): Port code to linux with compatible to different system and compiler. Add input command arguments, mass balance check,
binary output, control of enable/disable output and control of restart file. 

***New Features***
1. Add input command arguments, most of the parameters in parallel configuration file solver.cfg now can be read from the command arguments. 
2. Add relative mass balance tolerance to convergence check for flow simulation. 
3. Add openmp parallel section preprocessing definition 'PARALLEL_SECTION', if this flag is not used in compiling, the OpenMP parallel section is not activated.
4. Add make project for shared-memory version(OpenMP) and hybrid-distributed-shared memory version(MPI+OpenMP)
5. Add thread number option '-threadcomm_nthreads' and '-mpicomm_nthreads' at runtime. Use together with 'GLOBAL: USE NUMBER OF THREADS FROM MPI' in solver configuration file 'solver.cfg'
6. Add error information output when the input command does not meet the requirement, e.g., (1) vanleer spatial weighting requires stencil width 2 or larger, (2) GFortran cannot read non-standard string such as "str"ing.
7. Add binary input and output for restart file.
8. Add 'disable output of all gen files' to disable output of gen information.
9. Add 'enable output of master gen file' to output of gen information for the master thread.
10. Add binary output for spatial and transient output.
11. Add restart file read and write methods for different data format and storage types.
12. Add support for combining the zones in binary output for spatial data result. This is only valid when 'use binary format' is enabled and 'use separated file for spatial subdomain output' is disabled.
13. Add output precision control. Both double precision and single precision float point are supported in binary output.
14. Port codes to Linux with compatible to Intel Fortran, GFortran and IBM XL Compilers.

***Usage***
1. All the commands start with "-" and may be followed by an argument. Usage of input command: '-command_name' or '-command_name command_data', e.g., ./program.exe -input_file file_name -solver_type_flow 0 -solver_type_react 0 -numofthreads_global 4
2. Add 'mass balance convergence tolerance settings' followed by relative tolerance and absolute tolerance error to data block 'control parameters - variably saturated flow'. By default, the tolerance is set to a large value.
   E.g., 'mass balance convergence tolerance settings'
         1.0d-6      !relative mass balance convergence tolerance
         1.0d-8      !absolute mass balance convergence tolerance
3. Add 'PARALLEL_SECTION' to preprocessing definition. By default, this is not used.
4. N/A
5. Set 'GLOBAL: USE NUMBER OF THREADS FROM MPI' in solver configuration file or use command argument '-use_numofthreads_from_mpi' together with arguments '-threadcomm_nthreads' and '-mpicomm_nthreads'
   E.g., mpiexec -n 4 .\executable.exe -input_file file_name -use_numofthreads_from_mpi -mpicomm_nthreads 6. This will run the program using 4 processors, each processor will use 6 threads. Totally 24 cores will be used.
6. N/A
7. Add the following commands to "Data Block 1: global control parameters"

  'restart'                                  !Indicate whether to run from the restart file.
  'use distributed restart files to read'    !Indicate whether to use distributed restart files to read.
  'use distributed restart files to write'   !Indicate whether to use distributed restart files to write.
  'use binary restart file to read'          !Indicate whether to use binary restart file to read.
  'use binary restart file to write'         !Indicate whether to use binary restart file to write.
  'backup frequency'                         !Indicate the backup frequency.
  100                                        !Backup frequency 100 indicate write restart files every 100 time steps.
  
8. Add 'disable output of all gen files' to 'Data Block 1: global control parameters'

9. Add 'enable output of master gen file' to 'Data Block 1: global control parameters'

10. Add the following section to the input data file.

  ! Data Block 8: output control
  ! ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  !
  'output control'
  'use binary format'
  
11. Add the following section to the input file 'Data Block 1: global control parameters' 
  'restart'
  'use distributed restart files to read'    !restart files are distributed stored
  'use distributed restart files to write'   !restart files are distributed stored
  'use binary restart file to read'          !restart files are in binary format
  'use binary restart file to write'         !restart files are in binary format
12. By default, the binary output combines the multi-block zones (sub-domains) into one zone for spatial result output. 
   If you want to use multi-block zones output, add the command 'use multizone data format for spatial output' to 'Data Block 8: output control'. 
   E.g.,
   'output control'
   'use binary format'                               !use binary format
   'use separated file for spatial subdomain output' !files are separated by subdomains
   'use multizone data format for spatial output'    !use multi-block zones output

13. Add "OUTPUT_DOUBLE" to the preprocessor definition if "double precision output" is needed.
14. Add preprocessor definitions "STANDARD_FORTRAN", "PETSC_V3.5.X" in consistence with the system environment.
    #ifdef STANDARD_FORTRAN
        1 format(15x,100a15)    
        2 format(e15.5,100e15.7) 
    #else
        1 format(15x,<ncol>a15)    
        2 format(e15.5,<ncol>e15.7)    
    #endif 

***Updates***
1. Disable binary I/O in sequential version and OpenMP version. Binary output in sequential version and OpenMP version is not perfect.
2. Change output control for runtime. The global output control 'disable output' does not have any effect on runtime statistics output.
3. Change file name solver/solver_results.f90 to solver/solver_results.F90.
4. Update the openmp scheduling method in makefile to static for openmp version and hybrid mpi-openmp version
5. Modify makefile to exclude PETSc variables for sequential version and OpenMP version
6. Modify the number of maximum opening files to 65536, previous value is 4096.
7. Add convergence parameters output to screen and log file for Parallel version.
8. Update the output of gas advection in *.mac file, move the gas advection terms to the end.
9. Update to use MSHPC2012 and "PETSc for Windows 3.5.2"
10. Remove temporary storage (*.cnv, *.tmp) when the program terminates.
11. Replace the tab with four space '    ' in the codes. Tab-format lines are not part of the Fortran standard. It is an extension to Compaq/Intel's compiler. It is unlikely that other Fortran will support this format well. Usually, the compiler (e.g., GFortran) will give out "nonconforming tab character" warning.

***Bug Fixes***
1. Fix bug of race condition in infcrt_g.F90 and mbalrt.F90. This bug is introduced when gas advection is added.
2. Fix bug of uninitialized variable and array bound problem in OpenMP version. Need further consideration of array length n and nc when gas advection or compositional model is used.
3. Fix bug of debug build error and binary write when the output format is ascii format.
4. Fix bug of gas advection support for density dependent flow. Fix bug of .gsk output (gas advection) in OpenMP version. Change gas advection output extention name from .gsa to gsga.
5. Fix bug of unassigned values in vsflow when energy balance is considered for non-density-dependent flow. This bug exist for all MIN3P_THCm versions. It was first detected in the case co2_seq (CO2 sequestration in ultramafic tailings, Anna Harrison).
6. Fix bug of mass balance output (.mcd) for mcd problems. In code mbal_mcd.F90, the variables cfluxin_diff, cfluxin_mig, cfluxout_diff and cfluxout_mig should only be applied to boundary type first.
7. Fix bug of infinity value when divided by a small value. This bug exists in WestGrid Cluster.
8. Fix bug of restart simulation for transient source and updated boundary condition. Restart failed when the restart time is later than the target time of updating boundary condition.
9. Fix bug of gas component connection when 'sparse block matrices' option is used. For the previous version, this connection is considered for gas advection. But for the case 'nickel', this should also be considered.  
10. Fix bug of file unit allocation. The previous codes will cause file unit conflicts (ifort.xxx files) when the number of output of transient increases. Form this version, a file unit manage module is included.
11. Fix bug of 'driving force gradient' output in *.gtk files for diffusion problem. The previous output is always zero. This bug occurs when gas advection is used.
12. Fix bug of NAN output in viscosity output in _0.gsy as the mole fraction of gas phase is not calculated for the initial output, use zero instead.
13. Fix bug of uninitialization array when compiled using gfortran.
14. Fix bug of duplicated or missing tecplot header (*.mv*, *.mm*, *.ma*, *.ms*, *.mg*, *.mic, etc.
15. Fix bug of NaN value in vapor transport (.evap)
16. Fix bug of the output of *.hyc for the parallel version. Change to output local nodes only, without ghost nodes.
17. Fix bug of temp_n and visco_n output that are missing in full saturated flow when heat transport is considered in outputdd.F90 
18. Fix bug of velocity output for density dependent flow, the output includes 9 variables, not 6 variables. 
19. Fix bug of velocity output in velocity_a.F90, velocity_g.F90. Zone information is missing in the previous output.
20. Fix bug of initopgs.F90 when 'coordinate output' is used. This bug exists in parallel version only.
21. Fix bug in restart_r.F90. Call secspec(c, cx(ix,ivol), ...) should be call secspec(c(1,ivol), cx(ix,ivol), ...). This bug occurs in nwmo_basin simulations.

Revision: 259
Author: dsu
Date: November-19-14 4:39:52 PM
Message:
***Updates***
1. Add build flags for makefile for Westgrid clusters
2. Add convergence parameters output to screen and log file for Parallel version.

***Bug Fixes***
1.Fix bug of mpisubarray size 0 in parallel version for non-flow non-reactive transport problem.
2. Add error information output when the input command does not meet the requirement, e.g., (1) vanleer spatial weighting requires stencil width 2 or larger, (2) GFortran cannot read non-standard string such as "str"ing.
3. Fix bug of infinity value when divided by a small value. This bug exists in WestGrid Cluster.
4. Fix bug of restart simulation for transient source and updated boundary condition. Restart failed when the restart time is later than the target time of updating boundary condition.

Revision: 245
Author: dsu
Date: October-27-14 10:30:27 AM
Message:
***New Features***
1. Add binary input and output for restart file.
2. Add 'disable output of all gen files' to disable output of gen information.
3. Add 'enable output of master gen file' to output of gen information for the master thread.
4. Add binary output for batch reaction.

***Usage***
1. Usage of Feature 1: Add the following commands to "Data Block 1: global control parameters"

  'restart'                                  !Indicate whether to run from the restart file.
  'use distributed restart files to read'    !Indicate whether to use distributed restart files to read.
  'use distributed restart files to write'   !Indicate whether to use distributed restart files to write.
  'use binary restart file to read'          !Indicate whether to use binary restart file to read.
  'use binary restart file to write'         !Indicate whether to use binary restart file to write.
  'backup frequency'                         !Indicate the backup frequency.
  100                                        !Backup frequency 100 indicate write restart files every 100 time steps.
  
2. Usage of Feature 2: Add 'disable output of all gen files' to 'Data Block 1: global control parameters'

3. Usage of Feature 3: Add 'enable output of master gen file' to 'Data Block 1: global control parameters'

4. Add the following section to the input data file.

  ! Data Block 8: output control
  ! ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
  !
  'output control'
  'use binary format'

***Bug Fixes***
1. Fix bug of uninitialization array when compiled using gfortran. 

***Updates***
1. Update the restart file read and write commands to the following selection.
  'restart'
  'use distributed restart files to read'
  'use distributed restart files to write'
  'use binary restart file to read'
  'use binary restart file to write'
  'backup frequency'
  100

2. Update to use MSHPC2012 and "PETSc for Windows 3.5.2"

3. Update the output of gas advection in *.mac file, move the gas advection terms to the end.


***Notes***
1. Some of the tecplot headers/variables are changed (e.g., .mss in surfx), the tecplot layout should be changed accordingly.

2. Debug with Valgrind
mpiexec -n 1 valgrind --track-origins=yes --tool=memcheck -q --num-callers=20 --log-file=valgrind.log.%p ../../../min3p_thcm amd-ex

Revision: 242
Author: dsu
Date: October-09-14 5:51:50 PM
Message:
***New Features***
1. Add support for combining the zones in binary output for spatial data result. This is only valid when 'use binary format' is enabled and 'use separated file for spatial subdomain output' is disabled.

***Usage***
1. By default, the binary output combines the multi-block zones (sub-domains) into one zone for spatial result output. 
If you want to use multi-block zones output, add the command 'use multizone data format for spatial output' to 'Data Block 8: output control'. 


***Bug Fixes***
1. Fix bug in gsa binary output.
2. Fix bug in binary output of transient data when time steps between output is not 1.


***Updates***
1. Use 'use binary format' instead of 'output in binary format' in output control.
2. Use 'use separated file for spatial subdomain output' instead of 'output subdomain results to separated files'.

Revision: 238
Author: dsu
Date: September-30-14 11:55:45 AM
Message:
***New Features***
1. Add tecplot binary output for *.gs*, *.gb*, *.lb* files.
2. Add tecplot parallel binary output to single file for spacial output (*.gs*).
3. Add output precision. Both double precision and single precision float point are supported in binary output.

***Usage***
1. Add 'output in binary format' to "Data Block 8: output control" 
2. Add 'output subdomain results to separated files' if you want to export the results of different subdomains into separated files. By default, the results from different subdomains will be exported into a single file.
3. Add "OUTPUT_DOUBLE" to the preprocessor definition if "double precision output" is needed.


***Bug Fixes***
1. Fix bug in the output of *.hyc for the parallel version. Change to output local nodes only, without ghost nodes.
2. Fix bug of temp_n and visco_n output, where are missing in full saturated flow when heat transport is considered in outputdd.F90 
3. Fix bug in velocity output for density dependent flow, the output includes 9 variables, not 6 variables. 
4. Fix bug in velocity output in velocity_a.F90, velocity_g.F90. Zone information is missing in the previous output.
5. Fix bug in initopgs.F90 when 'coordinate output' is used. This bug exists in parallel version only.
6. Fix bug in duplicated output of tecplot header in *.mv*.

Revision: 230
Author: dsu
Date: August-21-14 5:04:11 PM
Message:
***Updates***
1. Port codes to Linux with compatible to Intel Fortran, GFortran and IBM XL Compilers.
2. Add preprocessor definitions "STANDARD_FORTRAN", "PETSC_V3.5.X"
 #ifdef STANDARD_FORTRAN
     1 format(15x,100a15)    
     2 format(e15.5,100e15.7) 
 #else
     1 format(15x,<ncol>a15)    
     2 format(e15.5,<ncol>e15.7)    
 #endif 
3. Remove some redundant codes.

***Notes***
 Codes sometimes fail on some clusters. The problem are detailed as follows:
1. All the simulations were tested with 8 to 256 processors. There is no problem on all clusters when using PETSc DEBUG version. The problem only occurs when using PETSc optimized version (-O3).
2. On Windows, build with Microsoft HPC package using Cygwin, or "PETSc For Windows 2.0". There is no problem in compiling and running up to 16 processor. I haven't test more than 16 processors as no Windows machine is available with more 16 processors.
3. On WestGrid Orcinus System (https://www.westgrid.ca/support/systems/Orcinus), there is no problem.
4. On WestGrid Jasper or Bugaboo System (https://www.westgrid.ca/resources_services),  the problem occurs. The codes may hang after many time steps, maybe 100 time steps, or maybe 200 time steps, like deadlock that all the processors are waiting. The simulation is terminated after the final walltime is reached. No error
----

Revision: 223
Author: dsu
Date: August-05-14 9:31:54 PM
Message:
***Updates***
1. Replace the tab with four space '    ' in the codes. Tab-format lines are not part of the Fortran standard. It is an extension to Compaq/Intel's compiler. It is unlikely that other Fortran will support this format well. Usually, the compiler (e.g., GFortran) will give out "nonconforming tab character" warning.

Branch to port codes to Linux/Unix platform, Part 2.

Added : /branches/dsu_linux_porting_2 (Copy from path: /trunk, Revision, 221)



***Summary of this branch (Revision 217 to 219): Make declaration of all the variables used in the codes.

***Updates***
1. Make declaration of all the variables. No implicit declaration is used any more.

***Bug Fixes***
1. Fix bugs in some un-defined variables.

***Notes***
The following variables are not set
1. i2upfind_heat.F90: rhalf, rho_z
2. initcpevaporation.F90: stabfac_atm

***Summary of this branch (revision 168 to 215 with other branches merged): Development of MIN3P parallel version using domain decomposition method.

***New Features***
1. Add domain decomposition method for flow and reactive transport simulation.
2. Add 'disable output' to control global output for parallel performance test.
3. Add support to read and write restart file for MPI parallel version. 
4. Modify dispersive/diffusive flux for mixed boundary condition. By default, dispersive/diffusive flux is applied from the boundary cells across the surface. 

***Usage***
1. Set the solver type to 2 and configure "Block:  PETSc solver setting" in solver configuration file.

   !> **********************************************************
   !>              Block:  PETSc solver setting
   !> **********************************************************
   !> Set this parameter if you want to check the result of matrix
   !> solver with ws209. Only valid if  i_solver_type = 0
   !> Requirement: Optional if you need to compare the result of
   !>              matrix solver.
   PETSC: SOLVER TEST WITH WS209
   
   !> Stencil width
   !> By default, the stencil width is 1, which can meet the requirements
   !> of most cases, but for the case with "vanleer" spatial weighting, 
   !> it will consider the second upstream point for flux limiter that the 
   !> stencil width should be 2 or more.
   PETSC: STENCIL WIDTH
   2
   
   !> Set this command to use the default configuration by PETSc.
   !> If you want to use the default solver configuration or 
   !> if you want to read the configuration from the command line,
   !> uncomment this command. Otherwise, comment this.
   PETSC: USE DEFAULT CONFIGURATION IN FLOW
   
   !> Set PETSc Krylov method for flow problem
   !> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
   !> KSPGMRES      "gmres"
   !> KSPBCGS       "bcgs"
   !> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
   !>              Required if use parallel mode
   !> Input example:
   !>               PETSC: KSP TYPE IN FLOW
   !>               kspgmres or gmres
   !> The default value is gmres
   PETSC: KSP TYPE IN FLOW
   kspgmres
   
   !> Set PETSc preconditioner type for flow problem
   !> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
   !> Algorithm          Name       Parallel
   !> PCNONE            "none"         N
   !> PCJACOBI          "jacobi"       Y
   !> PCLU              "lu"           N
   !> PCBJACOBI         "bjacobi"      Y
   !> PCILU             "ilu"          N
   !> PCASM             "asm"          Y
   !> PCKSP             "ksp"          Y
   !> PCHYPRE           "hypre"        Y
   !> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
   !>              Required if use parallel mode
   !> Input example:
   !>               PETSC: PRECONDITIONER TYPE IN FLOW
   !>               pcbjacobi or bjacobi
   !> The default value is bjacobi
   PETSC: PRECONDITIONER TYPE IN FLOW
   pcbjacobi
   
   !!> Set norm type
   !!>  KSP_NORM_NONE - skips computing the norm, this should only be used if you are using
   !!>                the Krylov method as a smoother with a fixed small number of iterations.
   !!>                Implicitly sets KSPSkipConverged as KSP convergence test.
   !!>                Supported only by CG, Richardson, Bi-CG-stab, CR, and CGS methods.
   !!>  KSP_NORM_PRECONDITIONED - the default for left preconditioned solves, uses the l2 norm
   !!>                of the preconditioned residual
   !!>  KSP_NORM_UNPRECONDITIONED - uses the l2 norm of the true b - Ax residual, supported only by
   !!>                CG, CHEBYSHEV, and RICHARDSON, automatically true for right (see KSPSetPCSide())
   !!>                preconditioning..
   !!>  KSP_NORM_NATURAL - supported  by KSPCG, KSPCR, KSPCGNE, KSPCGS
   !!PETSC: KSP NORM TYPE IN FLOW
   !!ksp_norm_preconditioned
   
   !> Set KSP solver convergence criteria
   !> By default, KSP solver will reach convergence when 
   !> rnorm < MAX (rtol * rnorm_0, abstol) or divergence if 
   !> rnorm > dtol * rnorm_0. The rnorm here is preconditioned 
   !> residual norm. If '-ksp_norm_type unpreconditioned' is used,
   !> rnorm is the true residual norm.
   !> If the solver convergence criteria is set to user-defined
   !> criteria, then the true residual will be calculated every
   !> iteration. The solver will reach convergence when 
   !> rnorm < MAX (rtol * rnorm_0, abstol).
   !> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
   !> KSPDEFAULT        "default"
   !> KSPUSERDEFINED    "userdefined"
   !> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
   !>              Required if use parallel mode
   !> Input example:
   !>               PETSC: KSP CONVERGENCE CRITERIA TYPE IN FLOW
   !>               kspdefault or default
   !> The default value is kspdefault
   PETSC: KSP CONVERGENCE CRITERIA TYPE IN FLOW
   kspdefault
   
   
   !> Set the relative convergence tolerance for flow problem
   !> The default value is 1.0E-5
   PETSC: RELATIVE CONVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN FLOW
   1.0E-5 
   !> Set the absolute convergence tolerance for flow problem
   !> The default value is 1.0E-20
   PETSC: ABSOLUTE CONVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN FLOW
   1.0E-20 
   !> Set the divergence tolerance for flow problem
   !> The default value is 1.0E5
   PETSC: DIVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN FLOW
   1.0E5
   !> Set the maximum number of iterations for flow problem
   !> The default value is 1000
   PETSC: MAXIMUM NUMBER OF ITERATIONS IN FLOW
   100
   
   !> Set this command to use the default configuration by PETSc.
   !> If you want to use the default solver configuration or 
   !> if you want to read the configuration from the command line,
   !> uncomment this command. Otherwise, comment this.
   PETSC: USE DEFAULT CONFIGURATION IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
   
   !> Set PETSc Krylov method for reactive transport problem
   !> The following methods are supported in PETSc
   !> KSPGMRES      "gmres"
   !> KSPBCGS       "bcgs"
   !> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
   !>              Required if use parallel mode
   !> Input example:
   !>               PETSC: KSP TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
   !>               kspgmres or gmres
   !> The default value is gmres
   PETSC: KSP TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
   kspgmres
   
   !> Set PETSc preconditioner type for reactive transport problem
   !> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
   !> Algorithm          Name       Parallel
   !> PCNONE            "none"         N
   !> PCJACOBI          "jacobi"       Y
   !> PCLU              "lu"           N
   !> PCBJACOBI         "bjacobi"      Y
   !> PCILU             "ilu"          N
   !> PCASM             "asm"          Y
   !> PCKSP             "ksp"          Y
   !> PCHYPRE           "hypre"        Y
   !> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
   !>              Required if use parallel mode
   !> Input example:
   !>               PETSC: PRECONDITIONER TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
   !>               pcbjacobi or bjacobi
   !> The default value is bjacobi
   PETSC: PRECONDITIONER TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
   pcbjacobi
   
   !!> Set norm type
   !!>  KSP_NORM_NONE - skips computing the norm, this should only be used if you are using
   !!>                the Krylov method as a smoother with a fixed small number of iterations.
   !!>                Implicitly sets KSPSkipConverged as KSP convergence test.
   !!>                Supported only by CG, Richardson, Bi-CG-stab, CR, and CGS methods.
   !!>  KSP_NORM_PRECONDITIONED - the default for left preconditioned solves, uses the l2 norm
   !!>                of the preconditioned residual
   !!>  KSP_NORM_UNPRECONDITIONED - uses the l2 norm of the true b - Ax residual, supported only by
   !!>                CG, CHEBYSHEV, and RICHARDSON, automatically true for right (see KSPSetPCSide())
   !!>                preconditioning..
   !!>  KSP_NORM_NATURAL - supported  by KSPCG, KSPCR, KSPCGNE, KSPCGS
   !!PETSC: KSP NORM TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
   !!ksp_norm_preconditioned
   
   !> Set KSP solver convergence criteria
   !> By default, KSP solver will reach convergence when 
   !> rnorm < MAX (rtol * rnorm_0, abstol) or divergence if 
   !> rnorm > dtol * rnorm_0. The rnorm here is preconditioned 
   !> residual norm. If '-ksp_norm_type unpreconditioned' is used,
   !> rnorm is the true residual norm.
   !> If the solver convergence criteria is set to user-defined
   !> criteria, then the true residual will be calculated every
   !> iteration. The solver will reach convergence when 
   !> rnorm < MAX (rtol * rnorm_0, abstol).
   !> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
   !> KSPDEFAULT        "default"
   !> KSPUSERDEFINED    "userdefined"
   !> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
   !>              Required if use parallel mode
   !> Input example:
   !>               PETSC: KSP CONVERGENCE CRITERIA TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
   !>               kspdefault or default
   !> The default value is kspdefault
   PETSC: KSP CONVERGENCE CRITERIA TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
   kspdefault
   
   !> Set the relative convergence tolerance for reactive transport problem
   !> The default value is 1.0E-5
   PETSC: RELATIVE CONVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
   1.0E-5 
   !> Set the absolute convergence tolerance for reactive transport problem
   !> The default value is 1.0E-20
   PETSC: ABSOLUTE CONVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
   1.0E-20 
   !> Set the divergence tolerance for reactive transport problem
   !> The default value is 1.0E5
   PETSC: DIVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
   1.0E5
   !> Set the maximum number of iterations for reactive transport problem
   !> The default value is 1000
   PETSC: MAXIMUM NUMBER OF ITERATIONS IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
   100

2. Add 'disable output' to "Data block 1: global control parameters"

3. When domain decomposition method is used, the restart file will be exported by different processors, with '_rank_n' as the indicator of the parallel restart file, e.g., restart_rank_0.tmp1, restart_rank_1.tmp1, .... The number of the restart files (number of processors) is also exported in the header of each restart file.If the restart data is a single file, you can run with the restart file without any change. If the restart data is distributed stored, e.g., restart_rank_0.tmp1, restart_rank_1.tmp1, ..., change the file extension file to .dat. Add the command 'distributed restart' to 'Data Block 1: global control parameters'. The number of processor used should be the same as the number of restart files, otherwise, there will be error showing that the number of restart file does not match. 

4. Add 'exclude diffusive flux from mixed boundary condition' to 'Data Block 16: boundary conditions - reactive transport'.
   
   e.g.,
    ! Data Block 16: boundary conditions - reactive transport
    ! ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    !
    'boundary conditions - reactive transport'
    2                             ;number of zones
    
    ! ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    'number and name of zone'
    1
    'inflow boundary'    
    'boundary type'
    'mixed'
    
    'exclude diffusive flux from mixed boundary condition'
    
    'concentration input'
    1.0000d-3   'free'         ;cl-1
    1.0000d-3   'free'         ;na+1
    1.8700d-3   'free'         ;o2(aq)
    ...

    !Note: When dispersive/diffusive flux is applied from the boundary cells, the results (*.gsc) of third boundary condition and mixed boundary condition cannot match. Exclude dispersive/diffusive flux for mixed boundary condition will solve this problem. 

***Updates***
1. Change the matrix extension file name from '.mtp' to '.mtx', the latter is the default suffix for MtxView software.

***Bad convergence benchmarks***
benchmarks_new_add\biomass-V1.0.56 batch1 - parallel
benchmarks_new_add\biomass-V1.0.56 batch2 - parallel

***Small difference***
1. benchmarks_standard\reactran\pyrox   It seems that the difference is caused by parallel round error
2. benchmarks_standard\reactran\raoult   organics-mass-log

***Deprecated benchmarks
1. Benchmarks_new_add\hMCD-V1.0.137. This benchmark is not backward compatible.

Revision: 196
Author: dsu
Date: May-23-14 1:47:04 PM
Message:
***Bug Fixes***
1. Fix bug for PETSc solver when the initial residual norm meet the convergence criteria. The previous version will return failure in solver.
2. Fix bug for mbalrt.F90 for MPI_AllReduce for dpdiffp and totdpdiffp
3. Fix bug for iajabl.F90, this bug will result in error matrix structure when fractional order terms are included, e.g, concrete-clay benchmark. This bug occurs from V177 for sparse matrix.
4. Fix bug for global and local node index map. 
5. Fix bug for density dependent flow in domain decomposition. PETSc use interlaced structure when dof > 1, in MIN3P, the reactive transport part uses interlaced structure but the density dependent flow use non-interlaced structure. The matrix and vector are converted from non-interlaced structure to interlaced structure for PETSc solver and then convert back to non-interlaced. To get a higher efficienty, it is better to modify the non-interlaced structure to interlaced structure.
----

Revision: 195
Author: dsu
Date: May-12-14 9:35:52 PM
Message:
***New Features***
1. Add support to read and write restart file for MPI parallel version. When domain decomposition method is used, the restart file will be exported by different processors, with '_rank_n' as the indicator of the parallel restart file, e.g., restart_rank_0.tmp1, restart_rank_1.tmp1, .... The number of the restart files (number of processors) is also exported in the header of each restart file.

***Usage***
1. If the restart data is a single file, you can run with the restart file without any change.
2. If the restart data is distributed stored, e.g., restart_rank_0.tmp1, restart_rank_1.tmp1, ..., change the file extension file to .dat. Add the command 'distributed restart' to 'Data Block 1: global control parameters'. The number of processor used should be the same as the number of restart files, otherwise, there will be error showing that the number of restart file does not match. 

----

***New Features***

  !> Set this command to use the default configuration by PETSc.

  !> uncomment this command. Otherwise, comment this.
  PETSC: USE DEFAULT CONFIGURATION IN FLOW


  !> Set this command to use the default configuration by PETSc.
  !> If you want to use the default solver configuration or 
  !> if you want to read the configuration from the command line,
  !> uncomment this command. Otherwise, comment this.
  PETSC: USE DEFAULT CONFIGURATION IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT

Revision: 170
Author: dsu
Date: March-13-14 3:20:03 PM
Message:
***New Feature***
1. Modify dispersive/diffusive flux for mixed boundary condition. By default, dispersive/diffusive flux is applied from the boundary cells across the surface. 

***Usage of New Feature***
1. Add 'exclude diffusive flux from mixed boundary condition' to 'Data Block 16: boundary conditions - reactive transport'.
   
   e.g.,
    ! Data Block 16: boundary conditions - reactive transport
    ! ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    !
    'boundary conditions - reactive transport'
    2                             ;number of zones
    
    ! ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    'number and name of zone'
    1
    'inflow boundary'    
    'boundary type'
    'mixed'
    
    'exclude diffusive flux from mixed boundary condition'
    
    'concentration input'
    1.0000d-3   'free'         ;cl-1
    1.0000d-3   'free'         ;na+1
    1.8700d-3   'free'         ;o2(aq)
    ...

***Note***
1. When dispersive/diffusive flux is applied from the boundary cells, the results (*.gsc) of third boundary condition and mixed boundary condition cannot match. Exclude dispersive/diffusive flux for mixed boundary condition will solve this problem. 
----

***Summary of this branch (revision 137 to 202): Development of the hybrid multicomponent diffusion model


Revision: 202
Author: mxie
Date: June-17-14 9:11:25 AM
Message:
hMCD model merged into the version 191.

New feature:
1. Development of the hybrid multicomponent diffusion (hMCD) model (final version).
1.	Add keyword 'hybrid multicomponent diffusion' in data Block 1: global control parameters to activate hMCD model.
2.	The correction factors of effective porosity and tortuosity for each primary and secondary species should be provided to each material group in Data Block 11: physical parameters - reactive transport. If no data is provided, default value of 1.0 will be applied, which means no modification.
3.	The correction factors of effective porosity of primary species are provided through the keyword ‘porosity correction factor of primary species for hMCD diffusion’ followed by the factors of each primary species in the same order as the components defined in Data Block 2: geochemical system under 'components'.
4.	The correction factors of effective porosity of secondary species are provided through the keyword ‘porosity correction factor of secondary species for hMCD diffusion’ followed by the factors of each secondary species in the same order as the species defined in Data Block 2: geochemical system under 'secondary aqueous species'.
5.	The correction factors of effective tortuosity of primary species are provided through the keyword ‘tortuosity correction factor of primary species for hMCD diffusion’ followed by the factors of each primary species in the same order as the components defined in Data Block 2: geochemical system under 'components'.
6.	The correction factors of effective tortuosity of secondary species are provided through the keyword ‘tortuosity correction factor of secondary species for hMCD diffusion’ followed by the factors of each secondary species in the same order as the species defined in Data Block 2: geochemical system under 'secondary aqueous species'.
7.	Example: The following example showed a 1D diffusion in radial coordinate. There are four materials – the circulation chamber, filter, gap and clay (‘background 1’). The hMCD model is applied only for the clay. The number of primary species is 17, while the number of secondary species is 23.

! Data Block 11: physical parameters - reactive transport
! ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
!
'physical parameters - reactive transport'

! ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
'circulation chamber'

'longitudinal dispersivity'
0.0
'transverse horizontal dispersivity'
0.0
'transverse vertical dispersivity'
0.0
'end of zone'

! ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
!
'filter'
'longitudinal dispersivity'
0.0
'transverse horizontal dispersivity'
0.0
'transverse vertical dispersivity'
0.0
'end of zone'

! ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
!
'gap'
'longitudinal dispersivity'
0.0
'transverse horizontal dispersivity'
0.0
'transverse vertical dispersivity'
0.0
'end of zone'

! ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
!
'background 1'
'longitudinal dispersivity'
0.0
'transverse horizontal dispersivity'
0.0
'transverse vertical dispersivity'
0.0

'porosity correction factor of primary species for hMCD diffusion'
  1.0    ;'cs+'  
  1.0    ;'na+1'
  1.0    ;'k+1'
  1.0    ;'mg+2'
  1.0    ;'ca+2'
  1.0    ;'sr+2' 
  0.667  ;'cl-1' 
  1.0    ;'h+1'
  1.0    ;'o2(aq)'
  0.667  ;'so4-2'
  0.667  ;'co3-1'
  0.533  ;'i-1' =0.08/0.15, effective porosity of I- is 0.08
  0.667  ;'br-1' 0.1/0.15, effective porosity of Br- is 0.10
  1.0    ;'HTO'
  1.0    ;'co_60+2'
  1.0    ;'eu+3'
  1.0    ;'sr_85+2'
  1.0    ;'=soh(s)'
  1.0    ;'=soh(w)'
  1.0    ;'=toh'

'tortuosity correction factor of primary species for hMCD diffusion'
  11.1    ;'cs+'
   1.0    ;'na+1'
   1.0    ;'k+1'
   1.0    ;'mg+2'
   1.0    ;'ca+2'
   1.0    ;'sr+2' 
   1.0    ;'cl-1' 
   1.0    ;'h+1'
   1.0    ;'o2(aq)'
   1.0    ;'so4-2'
   1.0    ;'co3-1'
  0.55    ;'i-1'
  0.38    ;'br-1'
   1.0    ;'HTO'
  3.0     ;'co_60+2'
   1.0    ;'eu+3'
   2.8    ;'sr_85+2'
   1.0    ;'=soh(s)'
   1.0    ;'=soh(w)'
   1.0    ;'=toh'

'porosity correction factor of secondary species for hMCD diffusion'
 1.0       	;'khso4(aq)'
 1.0       	;'h2so4(aq)'
 1.0        ;'oh-'
 1.0       	;'mgoh+'
 1.0       	;'mgco3aq'
 1.0       	;'mghco3+'
 1.0       	;'mgso4aq'
 1.0       	;'caoh+'
 1.0       	;'cahco3+'
 1.0       	;'caco3aq'
 1.0       	;'caso4aq'
 1.0       	;'cahso4+'
 1.0        ;'naco3-'
 1.0       	;'nahco3aq'
 1.0        ;'naso4-'
 1.0        ;'kso4-'
 1.0       	;'sroh+'
 1.0        ;'hco3-'
 1.0       	;'h2co3aq'
 1.0        ;'hso4-'
 1.0       	;'cscl(aq)'
 1.0       	;'csi(aq)'
 1.0       	;'csbr(aq)'

'tortuosity correction factor of secondary species for hMCD diffusion'
 1.0   		;'khso4(aq)'
 1.0   		;'h2so4(aq)'
 1.0   		;'oh-'
 1.0   		;'mgoh+'
 1.0   		;'mgco3aq'
 1.0   		;'mghco3+'
 1.0   		;'mgso4aq'
 1.0   		;'caoh+'
 1.0   		;'cahco3+'
 1.0   		;'caco3aq'
 1.0   		;'caso4aq'
 1.0   		;'cahso4+'
 1.0   		;'naco3-'
 1.0   		;'nahco3aq'
 1.0   		;'naso4-'
 1.0   		;'kso4-'
 1.0   		;'sroh+'
 1.0   		;'hco3-'
 1.0   		;'h2co3aq'
 1.0   		;'hso4-'
 1.0   		;'cscl(aq)'
 1.0   		;'csi(aq)'
 1.0   		;'csbr(aq)'


'end of zone'


'done'

Revision: 137
Author: mxie
Date: November-01-13 11:15:48 AM
Message:
***Summary of branch (Revision 131 and 136): Major work in this branch is the hybrid multicomponent diffusion model(hMCD).

xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx
xxx Summary of branch: Revision 131 and 136
xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

***New features***
1. hybrid multicomponent diffusion model(hMCD) (test implementation for Mt Terri project)

***Usage of new features***
1. Usage of feature:
   To use this function, two keywords are needed: 'effective porosity factor for MCD diffusion' and 'effective 
   tortuosity factor for MCD diffusion'in data block 11: physical parameters - reactive transport. This function 
   should be used under MCD (activated by 'multicomponent diffusion' under Datablock 1).
   The new input files can be found in the new-added benchmarks, see benchmarks_new_added\hMCD-V1.0.129. This 
   example test both multisite ion exchange and hMCD model.

1.1 Define effective porosity factor 
	
	'effective porosity factor for MCD diffusion'
    1.0    ;'cs+'  
    1.0    ;'na+1'
    1.0    ;'k+1'
    1.0    ;'mg+2'
    1.0    ;'ca+2'
    1.0    ;'sr+2'=0.15/0.11739 
    0.667   ;'cl-1' 'free'
    1.0    ;'h+1'
    1.0    ;'o2(aq)'
    0.667   ;'so4-2'
    0.667    ;'co3-1'
    0.533   ;'i-1' =0.08/0.15
    0.667   ;'br-1' 0.1/0.15
    1.0    ;'HTO'
    1.0    ;'co_60+2'
	
1.2 Define effective tortuosity factor 
    
    'effective tortuosity factor for MCD diffusion'
    1.0    ;'cs+'  
    1.0    ;'na+1'
    1.0    ;'k+1'
    1.0    ;'mg+2'
    1.0    ;'ca+2'
    3.5   ;1.277791975   ;1.0    ;'sr+2'=0.15/0.11739 
    1.0   ;'cl-1' 'free'
    1.0    ;'h+1'
    1.0    ;'o2(aq)'
    1.0     ;'so4-2'
    1.0     ;'co3-1'
    0.55   ;'i-1'
    0.38   ;'br-1'
    1.0    ;'HTO'
    300.0    ;'co_60+2'

	The name order should be the same as component. More details see the Mt Terri project report.

***Benchmarks Updates***
1. New benchmark added, see benchmarks_new_added\hMCD-V1.0.129.

***Bug fixes***
1. Fix bug of sorbed mass output for ion exchange reactions which may cause changes in the output file e.g _o.gen.

***Notes***
1. This approach is not fully implemented, i.e. material dependent factors is not considered.
----

Added : /branches/dsu_modify_shared_variable (Copy from path: /trunk, Revision, 178)



Summary of this branch (revision 171 to 176): Add gas advection iterm to MIN3P.

***New Features***
1. Add gas advection item to MIN3P. The gas advection item is ported from Dust Gas Model (DGM).
2. Add Openmp parallel control parameters for jacobi assembly in reactive transport.

***Usage***
1. Usage of Feature 1
1.1 Add the following options to 'Section 7: control parameters - reactive transport' to invoke the following functions:
   
   'gas advection'                - Include gas advection
   
   'cumulative mole fractions'    - Enable cumulative gas pressure value in the output .gsg.
   
   'enable gravity for gas phase' - Enable gravity for the gas phase, if enabled, grad = grad - dens * gacc * (zgj - zgi), where gpi and gpj are the gas pressure at the control volume i and j.

1.2. Add the following options to 'Section 11: physical parameters - reactive transport' to invoke the following functions:
   
   'update gas density'           -  Enable the option to update the gas density.
   
   'constant gas density'         - Use constant gas density. This commands should be followed by a new line with the gas density.
      E.g., 'constant gas density'
            1.29d+0
   
   'wilke viscosity'              - Use Wilke expression to compute gas viscosity value
      E.g.,  'wilke viscosity'
             2.04d-5         ;viscosity of gas component A
             1.46d-5         ;viscosity of gas component B
             1.75d-5         ;viscosity of gas component C
   
   'linear viscosity'             - Use linear expression to compute gas viscosity value
      E.g.,  'linear viscosity'
             2.04d-5         ;viscosity of gas component A
             1.46d-5         ;viscosity of gas component B
             1.75d-5         ;viscosity of gas component C
   
   'constant viscosity'           - Use constant gas viscosity value
      E.g.,  'constant viscosity'
             1.80d-5         ;viscosity of gas phase 

2. Usage of Feature 2
   Add the commands of parallel jacobi assembly for jacrt and jacbrt to "Block:  Matrix assembly setting" in parallel configuration file.
   
   E.g.,
   
   MATRIX ASSEMBLY: TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT 
   1
   MATRIX ASSEMBLY: TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT JACRT
   0    !Set jacrt to sequential version
   MATRIX ASSEMBLY: TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT JACBRT
   1    !Set jacbrt to parallel version
   
***Updates***
1. Update the default matrix export format to matrix market exchange format.

***Bug Fixes***
1. Fix bug in compiling error for the sequential version in debug mode. De-active the OpenMP function in this version.
2. Fix bug in update concentrations of surface sites, ulcmax = ulcmax/enat to ulcmax = ulcmax/dlog(r10)
3. Fix bug in sparse block matrices when dissolution-precipitation reactions are included.
4. Fix bug in updtbcatm.F90 when there are duplicated data in *.atm data set.
5. Fix bug in subroutine updtsvgp, new optional parameter eqg is added.

***Notes***
1. For the current version, the default block matrix format has been changed to 'sparse block matrices'. For some cases, if the solver does not converge well (with a lot of failing iterations), please increase the value of incomplete factorization level. By default, level 0 is used. Increasing this value to 1 or 3 will get better convergence.

Branch for implementation of gas advection.

Added : /branches/dsu_gas_advection (Copy from path: /trunk, Revision, 170)


Summary of this branch (revision 146 to 166): Main work for this branch is to add PETSc parallel solver for MIN3P and fix bug for 'sparse block matrices' option. It should be noted that the current version
is hybrid MPI and OpenMP. The solver can work for both MPI and OpenMP version while the matrix assembly can only work for OpenMP.

***New features***
1. Add PETSc parallel solver.
2. Set 'sparse block matrices' as the default option.
3. Add support to export matrix in Matrix Market (MM) exchange format.

***Usage of new features***
1. The following commands are used to configure PETSc solver.

    !> **********************************************************
    !>              Block:  Golbal solver setting
    !> **********************************************************
    !> This command indicates that the input parameters in the MIN3P input
    !> file have the priority over the input parameters in the parallel
    !> solver configuration file.
    !> By default, the parameters in this file will overwrite the same
    !> parameters in the MIN3P input file.
    !> For example, if there are solver convergence parameters in both 
    !> files, you can use the following command if you want to use MIN3P
    !> input parameters first.
    !> Requirement: Optional
    !> 
    GLOBAL: USE MIN3P INPUT PARAMETERS FIRST
    
    !>
    !> Use number of threads from MPI calling.
    !> If this command is enabled, the number of threads
    !> in OpenMP calling will be replaced by the number 
    !> of processors in mpi calling.
    GLOBAL: USE NUMBER OF THREADS FROM MPI

    !> **********************************************************
    !>              Block:  PETSc solver setting
    !> **********************************************************
    !> Set this parameter if you want to check the result of matrix
    !> solver with ws209. Only valid if  i_solver_type = 0
    !> Requirement: Optional if you need to compare the result of
    !>              matrix solver.
    PETSC: SOLVER TEST WITH WS209
    
    !> Set PETSc Krylov method for flow problem
    !> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
    !> KSPGMRES      "gmres"
    !> KSPBCGS       "bcgs"
    !> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
    !>              Required if use parallel mode
    !> Input example:
    !>               PETSC: KSP TYPE IN FLOW
    !>               kspgmres or gmres
    !> The default value is gmres
    PETSC: KSP TYPE IN FLOW
    kspgmres
    
    !> Set PETSc preconditioner type for flow problem
    !> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
    !> Algorithm          Name       Parallel
    !> PCNONE            "none"         N
    !> PCJACOBI          "jacobi"       Y
    !> PCLU              "lu"           N
    !> PCBJACOBI         "bjacobi"      Y
    !> PCILU             "ilu"          N
    !> PCASM             "asm"          Y
    !> PCKSP             "ksp"          Y
    !> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
    !>              Required if use parallel mode
    !> Input example:
    !>               PETSC: PRECONDITIONER TYPE IN FLOW
    !>               pcbjacobi or bjacobi
    !> The default value is bjacobi
    PETSC: PRECONDITIONER TYPE IN FLOW
    pcbjacobi
    
    !!> Set norm type
    !!>  KSP_NORM_NONE - skips computing the norm, this should only be used if you are using
    !!>                the Krylov method as a smoother with a fixed small number of iterations.
    !!>                Implicitly sets KSPSkipConverged as KSP convergence test.
    !!>                Supported only by CG, Richardson, Bi-CG-stab, CR, and CGS methods.
    !!>  KSP_NORM_PRECONDITIONED - the default for left preconditioned solves, uses the l2 norm
    !!>                of the preconditioned residual
    !!>  KSP_NORM_UNPRECONDITIONED - uses the l2 norm of the true b - Ax residual, supported only by
    !!>                CG, CHEBYSHEV, and RICHARDSON, automatically true for right (see KSPSetPCSide())
    !!>                preconditioning..
    !!>  KSP_NORM_NATURAL - supported  by KSPCG, KSPCR, KSPCGNE, KSPCGS
    !!PETSC: KSP NORM TYPE IN FLOW
    !!ksp_norm_preconditioned
    
    !> Set KSP solver convergence criteria
    !> By default, KSP solver will reach convergence when 
    !> rnorm < MAX (rtol * rnorm_0, abstol) or divergence if 
    !> rnorm > dtol * rnorm_0. The rnorm here is preconditioned 
    !> residual norm. If '-ksp_norm_type unpreconditioned' is used,
    !> rnorm is the true residual norm.
    !> If the solver convergence criteria is set to user-defined
    !> criteria, then the true residual will be calculated every
    !> iteration. The solver will reach convergence when 
    !> rnorm < MAX (rtol * rnorm_0, abstol).
    !> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
    !> KSPDEFAULT        "default"
    !> KSPUSERDEFINED    "userdefined"
    !> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
    !>              Required if use parallel mode
    !> Input example:
    !>               PETSC: KSP CONVERGENCE CRITERIA TYPE IN FLOW
    !>               kspdefault or default
    !> The default value is kspdefault
    PETSC: KSP CONVERGENCE CRITERIA TYPE IN FLOW
    kspdefault
    
    
    !> Set the relative convergence tolerance for flow problem
    !> The default value is 1.0E-5
    PETSC: RELATIVE CONVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN FLOW
    1.0E-5 
    !> Set the absolute convergence tolerance for flow problem
    !> The default value is 1.0E-20
    PETSC: ABSOLUTE CONVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN FLOW
    1.0E-20 
    !> Set the divergence tolerance for flow problem
    !> The default value is 1.0E5
    PETSC: DIVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN FLOW
    1.0E5
    !> Set the maximum number of iterations for flow problem
    !> The default value is 1000
    PETSC: MAXIMUM NUMBER OF ITERATIONS IN FLOW
    100
    
    !> Set PETSc Krylov method for reactive transport problem
    !> The following methods are supported in PETSc
    !> KSPGMRES      "gmres"
    !> KSPBCGS       "bcgs"
    !> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
    !>              Required if use parallel mode
    !> Input example:
    !>               PETSC: KSP TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
    !>               kspgmres or gmres
    !> The default value is gmres
    PETSC: KSP TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
    kspgmres
    
    !> Set PETSc preconditioner type for reactive transport problem
    !> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
    !> Algorithm          Name       Parallel
    !> PCNONE            "none"         N
    !> PCJACOBI          "jacobi"       Y
    !> PCLU              "lu"           N
    !> PCBJACOBI         "bjacobi"      Y
    !> PCILU             "ilu"          N
    !> PCASM             "asm"          Y
    !> PCKSP             "ksp"          Y
    !> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
    !>              Required if use parallel mode
    !> Input example:
    !>               PETSC: PRECONDITIONER TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
    !>               pcbjacobi or bjacobi
    !> The default value is bjacobi
    PETSC: PRECONDITIONER TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
    pcbjacobi
    
    !!> Set norm type
    !!>  KSP_NORM_NONE - skips computing the norm, this should only be used if you are using
    !!>                the Krylov method as a smoother with a fixed small number of iterations.
    !!>                Implicitly sets KSPSkipConverged as KSP convergence test.
    !!>                Supported only by CG, Richardson, Bi-CG-stab, CR, and CGS methods.
    !!>  KSP_NORM_PRECONDITIONED - the default for left preconditioned solves, uses the l2 norm
    !!>                of the preconditioned residual
    !!>  KSP_NORM_UNPRECONDITIONED - uses the l2 norm of the true b - Ax residual, supported only by
    !!>                CG, CHEBYSHEV, and RICHARDSON, automatically true for right (see KSPSetPCSide())
    !!>                preconditioning..
    !!>  KSP_NORM_NATURAL - supported  by KSPCG, KSPCR, KSPCGNE, KSPCGS
    !!PETSC: KSP NORM TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
    !!ksp_norm_preconditioned
    
    !> Set KSP solver convergence criteria
    !> By default, KSP solver will reach convergence when 
    !> rnorm < MAX (rtol * rnorm_0, abstol) or divergence if 
    !> rnorm > dtol * rnorm_0. The rnorm here is preconditioned 
    !> residual norm. If '-ksp_norm_type unpreconditioned' is used,
    !> rnorm is the true residual norm.
    !> If the solver convergence criteria is set to user-defined
    !> criteria, then the true residual will be calculated every
    !> iteration. The solver will reach convergence when 
    !> rnorm < MAX (rtol * rnorm_0, abstol).
    !> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
    !> KSPDEFAULT        "default"
    !> KSPUSERDEFINED    "userdefined"
    !> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
    !>              Required if use parallel mode
    !> Input example:
    !>               PETSC: KSP CONVERGENCE CRITERIA TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
    !>               kspdefault or default
    !> The default value is kspdefault
    PETSC: KSP CONVERGENCE CRITERIA TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
    kspdefault
    
    !> Set the relative convergence tolerance for reactive transport problem
    !> The default value is 1.0E-5
    PETSC: RELATIVE CONVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
    1.0E-5 
    !> Set the absolute convergence tolerance for reactive transport problem
    !> The default value is 1.0E-20
    PETSC: ABSOLUTE CONVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
    1.0E-20 
    !> Set the divergence tolerance for reactive transport problem
    !> The default value is 1.0E5
    PETSC: DIVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
    1.0E5
    !> Set the maximum number of iterations for reactive transport problem
    !> The default value is 1000
    PETSC: MAXIMUM NUMBER OF ITERATIONS IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
    100

2. If 'dense block matrices' is used in "Data Block 7: control parameters - reactive transport", then the system will use 'dense block matrices', otherwise, if 'sparse block matrices' is used or this option
is not specified, the system will use 'sparse block matrices'.

3. By default, the matrix will be exported in general 1D array. Set the output matrix format to 1 in parallel solver configuration file (solver.cfg) will export the matrix into MM format.
    !> Set the output matrix format  
    !> 0: export a (ia, ja), b, x as 1d array into separated files
    !> 1: export a (ia, ja) as matrix market exchange format, 
    !>    and b and x as 1d array into separated files
    !> Requirement: Optional, only for test.
    !> E.g.
    !> OUTPUT MATRIX FORMAT SELECTION
    !> 1    
    OUTPUT MATRIX FORMAT SELECTION
    1

***Updates***

***Benchmarks Updates***

***Bug fixes***
1. Fix bug of race condition caused by dtotg and ginc in jacbrt.F90.
2. Fix bug of exporting matrix when WatSolv is called. The exported matrix is exported after incomplete factorization. Exported the matrix before incomplete factorization.
3. Remove duplicated and null declaration in the codes. Some of these declaration will results into compiling error in IVF 14.0.
4. Fix bug that causes difference in the initial concentration of sorbed species in _0.gsb and .gen has been fixed. This bug is caused by the error pointer in gamma array in outputrt.F90. 
The previous codes use gamma (the same as gamma(1,1)) as the pointer address while the correct pointer address should be gamma(1, ivol). This bug exists from the initial version of min3p_thcm. 
For the archived benchmarks, the gamma(1,1) is the same as gamma(1, ivol) so that the result are still correct. But for concrete-clay benchmark, the bug is activated.

Message:
***New Features***
1. Add support for PETSc control parameters: ksp type, preconditioner type, convergence criteria type for both flow and reactive transport problem.

***Usage of New Features***
1. Add the following commands in solver configuration file (solver.cfg).

!> **********************************************************
!>              Block:  PETSc solver setting
!> **********************************************************
!> Set this parameter if you want to check the result of matrix
!> solver with ws209. Only valid if  i_solver_type = 0
!> Requirement: Optional if you need to compare the result of
!>              matrix solver.
PETSC: SOLVER TEST WITH WS209

!> Set PETSc Krylov method for flow problem
!> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
!> KSPGMRES      "gmres"
!> KSPBCGS       "bcgs"
!> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
!>              Required if use parallel mode
!> Input example:
!>               PETSC: KSP TYPE IN FLOW
!>               kspgmres or gmres
!> The default value is gmres
PETSC: KSP TYPE IN FLOW
kspgmres

!> Set PETSc preconditioner type for flow problem
!> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
!> PCNONE            "none"
!> PCJACOBI          "jacobi"
!> PCLU              "lu"
!> PCBJACOBI         "bjacobi"
!> PCILU             "ilu"
!> PCASM             "asm"
!> PCKSP             "ksp"
!> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
!>              Required if use parallel mode
!> Input example:
!>               PETSC: PRECONDITIONER TYPE IN FLOW
!>               pcbjacobi or bjacobi
!> The default value is bjacobi
PETSC: PRECONDITIONER TYPE IN FLOW
pcbjacobi

!!> Set norm type
!!>  KSP_NORM_NONE - skips computing the norm, this should only be used if you are using
!!>                the Krylov method as a smoother with a fixed small number of iterations.
!!>                Implicitly sets KSPSkipConverged as KSP convergence test.
!!>                Supported only by CG, Richardson, Bi-CG-stab, CR, and CGS methods.
!!>  KSP_NORM_PRECONDITIONED - the default for left preconditioned solves, uses the l2 norm
!!>                of the preconditioned residual
!!>  KSP_NORM_UNPRECONDITIONED - uses the l2 norm of the true b - Ax residual, supported only by
!!>                CG, CHEBYSHEV, and RICHARDSON, automatically true for right (see KSPSetPCSide())
!!>                preconditioning..
!!>  KSP_NORM_NATURAL - supported  by KSPCG, KSPCR, KSPCGNE, KSPCGS
!!PETSC: KSP NORM TYPE IN FLOW
!!ksp_norm_preconditioned

!> Set KSP solver convergence criteria
!> By default, KSP solver will reach convergence when 
!> rnorm < MAX (rtol * rnorm_0, abstol) or divergence if 
!> rnorm > dtol * rnorm_0. The rnorm here is preconditioned 
!> residual norm. If '-ksp_norm_type unpreconditioned' is used,
!> rnorm is the true residual norm.
!> If the solver convergence criteria is set to user-defined
!> criteria, then the true residual will be calculated every
!> iteration. The solver will reach convergence when 
!> rnorm < MAX (rtol * rnorm_0, abstol).
!> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
!> KSPDEFAULT        "default"
!> KSPUSERDEFINED    "userdefined"
!> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
!>              Required if use parallel mode
!> Input example:
!>               PETSC: KSP CONVERGENCE CRITERIA TYPE IN FLOW
!>               kspdefault or default
!> The default value is kspdefault
PETSC: KSP CONVERGENCE CRITERIA TYPE IN FLOW
kspdefault


!> Set the relative convergence tolerance for flow problem
!> The default value is 1.0E-5
PETSC: RELATIVE CONVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN FLOW
1.0E-5 
!> Set the absolute convergence tolerance for flow problem
!> The default value is 1.0E-20
PETSC: ABSOLUTE CONVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN FLOW
1.0E-20 
!> Set the divergence tolerance for flow problem
!> The default value is 1.0E5
PETSC: DIVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN FLOW
1.0E5
!> Set the maximum number of iterations for flow problem
!> The default value is 1000
PETSC: MAXIMUM NUMBER OF ITERATIONS IN FLOW
100

!> Set PETSc Krylov method for reactive transport problem
!> The following methods are supported in PETSc
!> KSPGMRES      "gmres"
!> KSPBCGS       "bcgs"
!> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
!>              Required if use parallel mode
!> Input example:
!>               PETSC: KSP TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
!>               kspgmres or gmres
!> The default value is gmres
PETSC: KSP TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
kspgmres

!> Set PETSc preconditioner type for reactive transport problem
!> The following methods are supported in PETSc
!> PCNONE            "none"
!> PCJACOBI          "jacobi"
!> PCLU              "lu"
!> PCBJACOBI         "bjacobi"
!> PCILU             "ilu"
!> PCASM             "asm"
!> PCKSP             "ksp"
!> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
!>              Required if use parallel mode
!> Input example:
!>               PETSC: PRECONDITIONER TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
!>               pcbjacobi or bjacobi
!> The default value is bjacobi
PETSC: PRECONDITIONER TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
pcbjacobi

!!> Set norm type
!!>  KSP_NORM_NONE - skips computing the norm, this should only be used if you are using
!!>                the Krylov method as a smoother with a fixed small number of iterations.
!!>                Implicitly sets KSPSkipConverged as KSP convergence test.
!!>                Supported only by CG, Richardson, Bi-CG-stab, CR, and CGS methods.
!!>  KSP_NORM_PRECONDITIONED - the default for left preconditioned solves, uses the l2 norm
!!>                of the preconditioned residual
!!>  KSP_NORM_UNPRECONDITIONED - uses the l2 norm of the true b - Ax residual, supported only by
!!>                CG, CHEBYSHEV, and RICHARDSON, automatically true for right (see KSPSetPCSide())
!!>                preconditioning..
!!>  KSP_NORM_NATURAL - supported  by KSPCG, KSPCR, KSPCGNE, KSPCGS
!!PETSC: KSP NORM TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
!!ksp_norm_preconditioned

!> Set KSP solver convergence criteria
!> By default, KSP solver will reach convergence when 
!> rnorm < MAX (rtol * rnorm_0, abstol) or divergence if 
!> rnorm > dtol * rnorm_0. The rnorm here is preconditioned 
!> residual norm. If '-ksp_norm_type unpreconditioned' is used,
!> rnorm is the true residual norm.
!> If the solver convergence criteria is set to user-defined
!> criteria, then the true residual will be calculated every
!> iteration. The solver will reach convergence when 
!> rnorm < MAX (rtol * rnorm_0, abstol).
!> The following methods are supported in PETSc:
!> KSPDEFAULT        "default"
!> KSPUSERDEFINED    "userdefined"
!> Requirement: Optional if use sequential mode
!>              Required if use parallel mode
!> Input example:
!>               PETSC: KSP CONVERGENCE CRITERIA TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
!>               kspdefault or default
!> The default value is kspdefault
PETSC: KSP CONVERGENCE CRITERIA TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
kspdefault

!> The default value is 1.0E-5
PETSC: RELATIVE CONVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
1.0E-5 
!> Set the absolute convergence tolerance for reactive transport problem

PETSC: ABSOLUTE CONVERGENCE TOLERANCE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT

Revision: 154
Author: dsu
Date: January-13-14 12:08:22 PM
Message:
***Bug Fix***
1. Bug that causes difference in the initial concentration of sorbed species in _0.gsb and .gen has been fixed. This bug is caused by the error pointer in gamma array in outputrt.F90. The previous codes use gamma (the same as gamma(1,1)) as the pointer address while the correct pointer address should be gamma(1, ivol). This bug exists from the initial version of min3p_thcm. For the archived benchmarks, the gamma(1,1) is the same as gamma(1, ivol) so that the result are still correct. But for concrete-clay benchmark, the bug is activated.

Revision: 146

Date: November-20-13 5:20:03 PM
Message:
This branch is created for MIN3P PETSc solver coding work.
----
Added : /branches/dsu_petsc_solver2 (Copy from path: /trunk, Revision, 145)



Summary of this branch (revision 107 to 143): Main work for this branch is to do OpenMP parallelization work for the sequential codes besides jacobi matrix assembly and solver.
This is based on the parallel performance analysis when number of threads is larger than 8 as the speedup will be deteriorated. 

***New features***
1. Add OpenMP parallel routine for the following codes:
initicrt.F90, initbcrt.F90, initicdd.F90, initbcenergybal.F90, restart_r.F90, batreac.F90, sptldisc.F90, xyzcoord.F90, cvolume.F90, mem_vs.F90, mem_heat.F90, mem_rt.F90, mem_dd.F90, mem_evap.F90, 
iajavs.F90, iajavs_dp.F90, iajavs_energybal.F90, mem_njavs.F90, mem_njart.F90, datstr_1.F90, datstr_n.F90, mem_mat.F90, initppvs.F90, initpppm.F90, initppenergybal.F90, initicvs_1.F90, initsatw.F90, 
initicenergybal.F90, updatedd.F90, updatedd_energybalance.F90, seepfdd.F90, seepface.F90, tstepvs.F90, updatevs.F90, soilparm.F90, msysdd.F90, mbaldd.F90, msysvs.F90, energysys.F90, energy_bal.F90, 
velodd.F90, nexttime.F90, infcvs.F90, comp_bc_ice_sheet.F90, m_ice_sheet.F90, infheat_c.F90, infheat_d.F90, timeloop.F90, infevap.F90, ddtds.F90, ddtds_energybal.F90, 
mbal_mcd.F90, tsteprt.F90, infcrtdd.F90, infcrt_a.F90, infcrt_g.F90, infcrt_mcd.F90, diffcoff_mcd.F90, i2upfind.F90, mbalrt.F90, msysrt.F90

2. Add OpenMP parallel control commands for the above parallel routines. This is mainly designed for parallel test or performance test.

3. Add executable file information (File name, Version, Copyright, and etc.) to executable file properties. The version number will be automatically replaced by VisualSVN revision number when the project is built.

4. Update trunk revision 129 and 137 to this branch.

5. Add MIN3P development guidelines to docs.

6. Add MIN3PRunAndResultCompare tool to tools.

***Usage of new features***
1. Usage of Feature 1 and 2: Add the following commands to enable/disable the parallel routine.

!> parallelization is enabled for this subroutine, otherwise,
!> if the threshold is larger than the loop amount, OpenMP
!> parallelization is disabled for this subroutine.
!> **********************************************************

1 


***Updates***
1. Modify the default backup frequency (restart file) from 10 to 100.
2. Modify writing of "Solver Iteration Convergence Summary" from debug file to log file.
3. Modify runtime calculation, the failed timestep is not included in *_nonlinear.prt but included in *_timestep.prt.
4. Preallocate memory space (iwork, rwork, afrt, afglob, ...) for the solver to improve performance as dynamic allocate memory space will seriously degrade performance for large scale simulation.
5. Update the rnorm_max threadhold for Pardiso solver. 
6. Update the output information for profiling test. The iteration parameters of Picard iteration, Newton iteration and seepage iteration are added. The previous "coupling time" is renamed as "nonlinear time".


***Benchmarks Updates***


***Bug fixes***
1. Fix bug in calling infcvs in restart_r.F90. This subroutine is not necessary to be called for every volume. This bug is benign and exists in all the previous versions. 
2. Fix bug in redundant calling tcorr in initprob. This bug is benign and exists in all the previous versions. 
3. Fix bug in output iteration number.


***Notes***
1. Pointer is treated as a shared variable in OpenMP. To avoid race condition, some of the pointer is removed and use allocatalbe array instead.
2. Pardiso vs iterative solver with ILU preconditioning such as PETSc: PETSc is an iterative solver whereas PARDISO direct one. Iterative methods with ILU preconditioned for good conditioner number converge faster than direct on the other hand direct return near exact solution. For ill condition matrix situation change a bit - converge of iterative solver become poor and direct method return solution quicker (its speed doesn't depend on condition number) but residual can be bad. These reasoning depend on preconditioner - so that's a hard to compare this 2 absolutely different approach of solving system of linear equation.

Revision: 143
Author: dsu
Date: November-19-13 11:07:55 AM
Message:
***Bug Fixes***
1. Fix bug in initprob.F90 for initialize maximum size of work array.
Previous codes: if (i_solver_type_react == 0 .or. i_solver_type_flow) then
Fixed codes: if (i_solver_type_react == 0 .or. i_solver_type_flow == 0) then
This bug only exist in this branch when i_solver_type_react == 1 and i_solver_type_flow == 0

2. Fix bug in calculating condition number for CYGWIN environment. As this calculation needs blas library, it is excluded in CYGWIN environment. The codes of this part can be activated by preprocessor definition CONDITION_NUMBER.

Revision: 120
Author: dsu
Date: September-23-13 3:10:19 PM
Message:
***Update***
1. Update the rnorm_max threadhold for Pardiso solver. 
Pardiso vs iterative solver with ILU preconditioning PETSc is an iterative solver whereas PARDISO direct one. Iterative methods with ILU preconditioned for good conditioner number converge faster than direct on the other hand direct return near exact solution. For ill condition matrix situation change a bit - converge of iterative solver become poor and direct method return solution quicker (its speed doesn't depend on condition number) but residual can be bad. These reasoning depend on preconditioner - so that's a hard to compare this 2 absolutely different approach of solving system of linear equation. 
----

Summary of branch (Revision 131 and 136): Major work in this branch is the hybrid multicomponent diffusion model(hMCD).

xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx
xxx Summary of branch: Revision 131 and 136
xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

***New features***
1. hybrid multicomponent diffusion model(hMCD) (test implementation for Mt Terri project)

***Usage of new features***
1. Usage of feature:
   To use this function, two keywords are needed: 'effective porosity factor for MCD diffusion' and 'effective 
   tortuosity factor for MCD diffusion'in data block 11: physical parameters - reactive transport. This function 
   should be used under MCD (activated by 'multicomponent diffusion' under Datablock 1).
   The new input files can be found in the new-added benchmarks, see benchmarks_new_added\hMCD-V1.0.137. This 
   example test both multisite ion exchange and hMCD model.

1.1 Define effective porosity factor 
	
	'effective porosity factor for MCD diffusion'
    1.0    ;'cs+'  
    1.0    ;'na+1'
    1.0    ;'k+1'
    1.0    ;'mg+2'
    1.0    ;'ca+2'
    1.0    ;'sr+2'=0.15/0.11739 
    0.667   ;'cl-1' 'free'
    1.0    ;'h+1'
    1.0    ;'o2(aq)'
    0.667   ;'so4-2'
    0.667    ;'co3-1'
    0.533   ;'i-1' =0.08/0.15
    0.667   ;'br-1' 0.1/0.15
    1.0    ;'HTO'
    1.0    ;'co_60+2'
	
1.2 Define effective tortuosity factor 
    
    'effective tortuosity factor for MCD diffusion'
    1.0    ;'cs+'  
    1.0    ;'na+1'
    1.0    ;'k+1'
    1.0    ;'mg+2'
    1.0    ;'ca+2'
    3.5   ;1.277791975   ;1.0    ;'sr+2'=0.15/0.11739 
    1.0   ;'cl-1' 'free'
    1.0    ;'h+1'
    1.0    ;'o2(aq)'
    1.0     ;'so4-2'
    1.0     ;'co3-1'
    0.55   ;'i-1'
    0.38   ;'br-1'
    1.0    ;'HTO'
    300.0    ;'co_60+2'

	The name order should be the same as component. More details see the Mt Terri project report.

***Benchmarks Updates***
1. New benchmark added, see benchmarks_new_added\hMCD-V1.0.137.

***Bug fixes***
1. Fix bug of sorbed mass output for ion exchange reactions which may cause changes in the output file e.g _o.gen.

***Notes***
1. This approach is not fully implemented, i.e. material dependent factors is not considered.



Summary of branch (Revision 124 and 128): Major work in this branch is the multisite ion-exchange model.

xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx
xxx Summary of branch: Revision 124 and 128
xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

***New features***
1. Multisite ion-exchange reaction for Gaines Thomas convention

***Usage of new features***
1. Usage of feature:
   To use this function, two keywords are needed: 'surface sites of ion-exchange' in data block 2: geochemical 
   system and 'CEC fraction of multisite ion exchange' in Data Block 14: initial condition - reactive transport 
   for the specification of the amount of each site.
   The new input files can be found in the new-added benchmarks, see benchmarks_new_added\multisite-ionx-V1.0129.

1.1 Define number and names of surface sites and sorbed species, add the following input command/data to Data 
	Block 2: geochemical system. 
	
	'surface sites of ion-exchange'
	2
	'-x'
	'-Y'
	
	'sorbed species of ion-exchange'
	12
	'mg-x(na)'
	'na-x(na)'
	'k-x(na)'
	'zn-x(na)'
	'pb-x(na)'
	'ca-x(na)'
	'mg-Y(na)'
	'na-Y(na)'
	'k-Y(na)'
	'zn-Y(na)'
	'pb-Y(na)'
	'ca-Y(na)'
	
	The name of each site should include ‘-‘. The sorbed species name has to use the site name plus (na). 
	For example mg-Y(na) stands for the sorbed species of Mg+2 on –Y and exchanges with Na+. The exact reaction 
	will be defined in the database in the same way for single site ion excahnge reactions (see user manual).

1.2 Define the amount of each sites, add the following input command/data to Data Block 14: initial 
	condition - reactive transport
	
	'sorption parameter input of ion-exchange'
	0.733                      		;total cation exchange capacity (CEC) [meq/100 g solid]
	1.875d0                        	;dry bulk density [g/cm^3]
	
	'CEC fraction of multisite ion exchange'
	0.9                         	;'-x'
	0.1                           	;'-Y'
	
	The input format and units for the total CEC value and dry bulk density parameters remain the same as the
	single site ion excahnge reaction. But the keyword must be updated as the one in revision 94 as shown above.
	The amount of each sites are specified by the CEC fractions. They should be given in the same order as given
	under the keyword 'surface sites of ion-exchange'. The total CEC fraction should be 1.0. 


***Benchmarks Updates***
1. New benchmark added, see benchmarks_new_added\multisite-ionx-V1.0129.

***Bug fixes***
1. Fix bug of pe, pH and Eh initialization which may cause NaN value in the output file e.g *.gbm, *.gsm, phpe.F90.

***Notes***
1. Multisite ion-exchange reaction for Gapon convention is not verified yet.


xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx
xxx Summary of branch: Revision 95 to 106
xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

***New features***
1. Separation of surface-complex and ion-exchange reaction so as to support both reactions at the same time.
2. Output the matrix data and right hand side for the linear equations. This is only used for solver testing.

***Usage of new features***
1. Usage of feature 1:
   If only surface-complex or ion-exchange is considered, you can just use the previous input file. 
   If both surface-complex and ion-exchange is considered, you should use the following input format.
   You can also modify the following input file for only surface-complex or ion-exchange, just delete the unnecessary part.
   The new input files can be found in the new-added benchmarks, see benchmarks_new_added\surfx-ionx-V1.0.106.

1.1 Define sorption type, add the following input command/data to Data Block 2: geochemical system. 
	
	'define sorption type of surface-complex'
	'surface-complex'
	
	'define sorption type of ion-exchange'
	'gaines-thomas'

1.2 Define sorbed species, add the following input command/data to Data Block 2: geochemical system.
	
	'sorbed species of surface-complex'
	3                         
	'=feoh2+(s)'                  
	'=feo-(s)'
	'=feozn+(s)'
	
	'sorbed species of ion-exchange'
	3                           
	'na-x(na)'
	'ca-x(na)v'
	'mg-x(na)v'

1.3 Define sorption parameter, add the following input command/data to Data Block 14: initial condition - reactive transport
	
	'sorption parameter input of ion-exchange'
	10.0d0      ;cation exchange capacity [meq/100 g solid]; ion-exchange part, cec
	1.875d0      ;dry bulk density [g/cm^3]; ion-exchange part, rhobulk
	
	'sorption parameter input of surface-complex'
	'=feoh(s)'  100.0d0  10.0d0  6.02228d0  ;surface site, mass, area, site density; surface-complex part
	
1.4 Define equilibrate type, add the following input command/data to Data Block 14: initial condition - reactive transport
	
	'equilibrate with fixed solution composition of ion-exchange
	!'equilibrate with fixed solution composition of surface-complex'

2. Usage of feature 2
	Add the following input command/data to parallel solver configuration file (e.g, solver.cfg)  
	!> Set if output the matrix data for all the linear equations.
	!> Comment out if you do not want to export it.
	!> Requirement: Optional, only for test.
	OUTPUT SPARSE MATRIX DATA SET AND RHS

***Updates***
1. Add schedule 'guided' for parallel schedule.
2. Update the format of long names output in gen file. For the 72-character long names, export the first 12 characters if the name is followed by some other data in the same line.

***Benchmarks Updates***
1. New benchmark added, see benchmarks_new_added\surfx-ionx-V1.0.106.

***Bug fixes***
1. Fix bug of race condition in ddbdflux.F90.

***Notes***
1. There is some difference between MIN3P and Phreeqc when both surface complex and ion exchange are considered, further check may be needed.
