Revision: 98
Author: dsu
Date: June-20-13 12:03:18 PM
Message:
***New Feature***
1. Add input commands for 'surface-complex and ion-exchange'

***Usage***
1. 'define sorption type' 
Add the following commands to 'Data Block 2: geochemical system' if you want to use the sorption of 'surface-complex' or 'surface-complex and ion-exchange'.

  'define sorption type'
  'surface-complex and ion-exchange'

2. 'sorbed species'
Add the following commands to 'Data Block 2: geochemical system'.

  'sorbed species of surface-complex and ion-exchange'
  3   2                     ; surface-complex and ion-exchange      
  '=feoh2+(s)'                  
  '=feo-(s)'
  '=feozn+(s)'
  'na-x(na)'
  'ca-x(na)v'

The first number 3 is the number of sorbed species for surface-complex, the second number 2 is the number of sorbed species for ion-exchange. If the number of sorbed species is zero, put 0 in the data line, e.g., 

  'sorbed species of surface-complex and ion-exchange'
  3   0                     ; surface-complex only     
  '=feoh2+(s)'                  
  '=feo-(s)'
  '=feozn+(s)'

  'sorbed species of surface-complex and ion-exchange'
  0   2                     ; ion-exchange only     
  'na-x(na)'
  'ca-x(na)v'

If the defined sorption type is 'surface-complex and ion-exchange', please USE 'sorbed species of surface-complex and ion-exchange' instead of 'sorbed species', otherwise, there will be unexpected error.

3. 'sorption parameter input'
The previous commands for 'sorption parameter input' has been modified to read both surface-complex and ion-exchange sorption parameters. The ion-exchange sorption parameter should be put after the surface-complex parameter. 

  'sorption parameter input'
  10.0d0      ;cation exchange capacity [meq/100 g solid]; ion-exchange part, cec
  1.875d0      ;dry bulk density [g/cm^3]; ion-exchange part, rhobulk
  '=feoh(s)'  100.0d0  10.0d0  6.02228d0  ;surface site, mass, area, site density; surface-complex part

You can also use separated 'sorption parameter input' commands for 'surface-complex' and 'ion-exchange' respectively. The commands and example input are as follows:

  'sorption parameter input of ion-exchange'
  10.0d0      ;cation exchange capacity [meq/100 g solid]; ion-exchange part, cec
  1.875d0      ;dry bulk density [g/cm^3]; ion-exchange part, rhobulk

  'sorption parameter input of surface-complex'
  '=feoh(s)'  100.0d0  10.0d0  6.02228d0  ;surface site, mass, area, site density; surface-complex part



***Bug Fix***
1. Fix bug for 'surface-complex and ion-exchange' input.

***Note***
1. If the defined sorption type is 'surface-complex and ion-exchange', please USE 'sorbed species of surface-complex and ion-exchange' instead of 'sorbed species', otherwise, there will be unexpected error.

xxx Summary of branch: Revision 68 to 93
xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

***New features***
1. Add parallel version of matrix assembly and solver for MIN3P.
2. Add condition number estimation module.
3. Update the length of name from 12 to 72.
4. Add build environment to the log file when the program runs.


***Usage of new features***

**Usage of parallel version of MIN3P**
For the formatted user guide for parallel version of MIN3P, please look into MIN3P_THCm_User_Manual_Parallel.doc.

1	PARALLEL CONFIGURATION
Parallel Configuration is used to configure the system environment and input parameters. The configuration focuses on the parallel matrix solver, parallel matrix assembly, parallel performance analysis and testing. 

1.1	SYSTEM REQUIREMENT
Redistributable libraries are needed for the OS without developing environment. Currently, the program is compiled with Intel Visual Fortran compiler. Before running the parallel version of MIN3P, please install Intel(R) Visual Redistributables on the system first. 
1.1.1	System requirement for Windows OS

For 32-bit OS, please install Intel(R) Visual Fortran Redistributables on IA-32; For 64-bit OS, please install Intel(R) Visual Fortran Redistributables on 64.

The Redistributable libraries corresponding to the developing environment can be download via the URL
http://software.intel.com/en-us/articles/redistributable-libraries-for-intel-c-and-visual-fortran-composer-xe-2013-for-windows.

1.1.2	System requirement for Linux OS
(To be added … )

1.2	CONFIGURATION FILE ENTRY
To use parallel configuration file, please add the following command and data in the block of “Data Block 1: global control parameters” in MIN3P input file. 

'parallel solver configuration file'
'solver.cfg'

Where 'solver.cfg' is path of configuration file. 

1.3	CONFIGURATION FILE FORMAT
The entire configuration is saved in an ASCII text file (e.g., parallel.cfg). The line begin with exclamation mark (!) is a comment line. The character is NOT case-sensitive. The line begin with alphabet character is a command line or a data line. The line begin with number is a data line. Not all the command line or data line is required. The latter command will overwrite the former command if they have the same target.

1.4	GLOBAL SOLVER SETTING
Global solver setting includes the solver type for flow and reactive transport, as well as the number of threads for global use. 
There are two solvers in the parallel version of MIN3P. The first solver is WatSolv (or WS209), which is the default solver of MIN3P. WatSolv is partially parallelized. The second solver is Pardiso, which is a shared-memory directly solver.

1.4.1	Command and data
1.4.1.1	Solver type
Set solver type for flow and reactive transport equations. Solver type is given by a command line and a data line.

solver type
i

Set solver type for both flow equations and reactive transport equations. If i is 0, use WatSolv as the solver, if i is 1, use Pardiso as the solver. If this command is not specified, the default solver is WatSolv.

solver type flow
i

Set solver type for flow equations only. If i is 0, use WatSolv as the solver, if i is 1, use Pardiso as the solver. If this command is not specified, the default solver is WatSolv.


solver type reactive transport
i

Set solver type for reactive transport equations only. If i is 0, use WatSolv as the solver, if i is 1, use Pardiso as the solver. If this command is not specified, the default solver is WatSolv.

1.4.1.2	Number of threads
Set the number of threads for global use, including number of threads for matrix assembly, parallel solver as well as other parallel region.

global: number of threads
n

Set the number of threads n for global use. The number of threads for global use includes matrix assembly, matrix solver and other parallel region. If this command is not specified, the default number of threads for global use is 1.


1.4.2	Sample configuration

!> **********************************************************
!>              Block:  Golbal solver setting
!> **********************************************************
!SOLVER TYPE
!1

SOLVER TYPE FLOW
0

SOLVER TYPE REACTIVE TRANSPORT
1

GLOBAL: NUMBER OF THREADS
4  


1.5	MATRIX ASSEMBLY SETTING
Matrix assembly setting includes matrix assembly type, number of threads used for matrix assembly, and chunk size factor. Matrix assembly setting is separated for flow and reactive transport. If this part is not specified, and the number of threads for global use is 1, then the system will run in sequential mode for matrix assembly.

1.5.1	Command and data
1.5.1.1	Matrix assembly type
Set matrix assembly type for flow and reactive transport equations. Matrix assembly type is given by a command line and a data line.

matrix assembly: type in flow
i

Set matrix assembly type for flow equations only. If i is 0, use sequential mode (non-parallel) for matrix assembly, if i is 1, use OPENMP parallel mode for matrix assembly. If this command is not specified, the default mode is sequential mode.

matrix assembly: type in reactive transport
i

1.5.1.2	Number of threads
Set matrix assembly type for reactive transport equations only. If i is 0, use sequential mode (non-parallel) for matrix assembly, if i is 1, use OPENMP parallel mode for matrix assembly. If this command is not specified, the default mode is sequential mode.

matrix assembly: number of threads in flow
n

Set the number of threads n in matrix assembly for flow equations only. If not specified, use 'global: number of threads' instead. 

matrix assembly: number of threads in reactive transport
n

Set the number of threads n in matrix assembly for reactive transport equations only. If not specified, use 'global: number of threads' instead. 

1.5.1.3	Schedule type
matrix assembly: schedule type in flow
i

Set the schedule type in matrix assembly for flow equations only. If i is 0, use static schedule method. If i is 1, use dynamic schedule method. The default value is 0. Currently schedule type is pre-defined in compiling option. DO NOT use the setting here.

matrix assembly: schedule type in reactive transport
i

Set the schedule type in matrix assembly for reactive transport equations only. If i is 0, use static schedule method. If i is 1, use dynamic schedule method. The default value is 0. Currently schedule type is pre-defined in compiling option. DO NOT use the setting here.

1.5.1.4	Chunk size in parallel loop
matrix assembly: chunk size factor in flow
n

Set the chunk size factor for matrix assembly for flow equations only. This value should be from 0 to ‘(loop count)/(number of processors)’.If the value is not specified or 0, use the system default value. The default value depends on the schedule type.

matrix assembly: chunk size factor in reactive transport
n

Set the chunk size factor for matrix assembly for reactive equations only. This value should be from 0 to ‘(loop count)/(number of processors)’.If the value is not specified or 0, use the system default value. The default value depends on the schedule type.

1.5.2	Sample configuration

!> **********************************************************
!>              Block:  Matrix assembly setting
!> **********************************************************


MATRIX ASSEMBLY: TYPE IN FLOW
1

MATRIX ASSEMBLY: NUMBER OF THREADS IN FLOW
4

MATRIX ASSEMBLY: SCHEDULE TYPE IN FLOW
0

MATRIX ASSEMBLY: CHUNK SIZE FACTOR IN FLOW
0


MATRIX ASSEMBLY: TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT 
1

MATRIX ASSEMBLY: NUMBER OF THREADS IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
4

MATRIX ASSEMBLY: SCHEDULE TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
0

MATRIX ASSEMBLY: CHUNK SIZE FACTOR IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
0


1.6	PARDISO SOLVER SETTING
PARDISO is a thread-safe, high-performance, robust, memory efficient and easy to use software for solving large sparse symmetric and unsymmetric linear systems of equations on shared-memory. The following settings are valid only if the solver type is Pardiso.

1.6.1	Command and data
1.6.1.1	Solver test
pardiso: solver test with ws209

Set this command if you want to check the result of matrix solver with WatSolv (WS209). This command is valid only if the solver type is WatSolv. Do not include this command in the normal use. This is only used for testing.

1.6.1.2	Number of Threads
pardiso: number of threads
n

Set the number of threads n for pardiso solver. This is valid only if the solver type is Pardiso. If the number of threads is 0, use number of threads determined by pardiso, which is uaually the number of physical cores.

1.6.1.3	Solver refinement
pardiso: max iterative refinement steps in flow
n

pardiso: max iterative refinement steps in reactive transport
n

Set the maximum number of iterative refinement steps n that the solver will perform for flow equations and reactive transport equations, respectively. The solver will perform not more than the absolute value of this parameter for iterative refinement and will stop the process if a satisfactory level of accuracy of the solution in terms of backward error has been achieved.If this parameter is negative, the accumulation of the residuum is using extended precision real types. The default value is 9.

1.6.1.4	Pivoting perturbation
pardiso: pivoting perturbation in flow
n

pardiso: pivoting perturbation in reactive transport
n

This parameter controls how to handle small pivots or zero pivots for unsymmetric matrices for flow equations and reactive transport equations, respectively. It indicates the iterative refinement contraction rate. The default value is 13, which means eps = 10^(-13) is used in handling small pivots.

1.6.1.5	Preconditioning
pardiso: cgs criterion in flow
n

pardiso: cgs criterion in reactive transport
n

This parameter controls preconditioned CGS [sonn89] for nonsymmetric matrices for flow equations and reactive transport equations, respectively. The parameter n has the form 10*l+k.If k=0, the factorization is always computed as required by phase. If k=1 CGS iteration replaces the computation of LU. The preconditioner is LU that was computed at a previous step (the first step or last step with a failure) in a sequence of solutions needed for identical sparsity patterns. For example, if n is 31, the solver will use LU-preconditioned CGS iteration with a stopping criterion of 1.0e-3 for nonsymmetric matrices ; If n is 61, the solver will use LU-preconditioned CGS iteration with a stopping criterion of 1.0e-6 for nonsymmetric matrices. The default value is 0.

1.6.1.6	Maximum solver iteration
pardiso: maximum solver iteration in flow
n

pardiso: maximum solver iteration in reactive transport   
n

This parameter controls the maximum solver iteration n for flow equations and reactive transport equations, to see if symbolic factorization should take before the next iteration. In pardiso solver, when the previous reordering is not quite good to get correct results, reorder the matrix (symbolic factorization) again. For nonsymmetric case, it's better to call reorder step for each matrix, but this can waste a lot of time. When preconditioned CGS is used, this value will be compared to the number of completed iterations, otherwise, this value will be compared to the number of iterative refinement steps performed. When the iteration number is larger than the specified value, do symbolic factorization again. The default value is 9.

1.6.1.7	Maximum residual
pardiso: maximum residual in flow
e

pardiso: maximum residual in reactive transport
e

This parameter controls the maximum residual e for flow equations and reactive transport equations, to see if symbolic factorization should take before the next iteration. In pardiso solver, when the previous reordering is not quite good to get correct results, reorder the matrix (symbolic factorization) again. For nonsymmetric case, it's better to call reorder step for each matrix, but this can waste a lot of time. When the maximum residual is larger than the specified value, do symbolic factorization again. The default value is 1.0e-5.

1.6.1.8	Symbolic factorization type
pardiso: symbolic factorization type in flow
i

pardiso: symbolic factorization type in reactive transport
i

This parameter controls the symbolic factorization type i for flow equations and reactive transport equations. If the symbolic factorization type is 0, do symbolic factorization only once before the iteration. If the symbolic factorization type is 1, do symbolic factorization at every newton iteration step. When symbolic factorization type is 0, the “matrix solver iteration” and “maximum residual” parameters are valid.

1.6.2	Tips on pardiso solver
Pardiso cannot handle ill-conditioned matrix. For some nearly ill-conditioned matrices, pardiso solver may generate inaccurate results in solving equations that cause failure in Newton iteration.   If Newton iteration fails, try to use WatSolv solver. You can also output the condition number to see if the matrix is ill-conditioned. 

1.6.3	Sample configuration
!> **********************************************************
!>              block:  pardiso solver setting
!> **********************************************************

! PARDISO: SOLVER TEST WITH WS209

PARDISO: NUMBER OF THREADS
0

PARDISO: MAX ITERATIVE REFINEMENT STEPS IN FLOW
9

PARDISO: MAX ITERATIVE REFINEMENT STEPS IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
9

PARDISO: PIVOTING PERTURBATION IN FLOW
13

PARDISO: PIVOTING PERTURBATION IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
13

PARDISO: CGS CRITERION IN FLOW
0

PARDISO: CGS CRITERION IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
0

PARDISO: MAXIMUM SOLVER ITERATION IN FLOW
5
PARDISO: MAXIMUM SOLVER ITERATION IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT   
5

PARDISO: MAXIMUM RESIDUAL IN FLOW
1.0E-8
PARDISO: MAXIMUM RESIDUAL IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
1.0E-8

PARDISO: SYMBOLIC FACTORIZATION TYPE IN FLOW
0

PARDISO: SYMBOLIC FACTORIZATION TYPE IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
0

1.7	WATSOLV SOLVER SETTING
For the present version, WatSolv (WS209) is not fully parallelized. Only part of the loops in the solver is parallelized. The only parameters that can be controlled is the number of threads.

1.7.1	Command and data

1.7.1.1	Number of Threads
ws209: number of threads
n

Set the number of threads n for WatSolv (WS209). This is valid only if the solver type is WatSolv. If not specified, use the default value 1 as the number of threads.

1.7.2	Sample configuration
!> **********************************************************
!>              block:  ws209 solver setting
!> **********************************************************
WS209: NUMBER OF THREADS
4

1.8	OUTPUT SETTING
1.8.1	Command and data
1.8.1.1	Runtime statistics analysis
output runtime statistics analysis

This command outputs the runtime profiling, including runtime of matrix assembly, factorization and substitution in each Newton iteration step, runtime of flow and reactive transport in each time step. The output file is in tecplot data format.

1.8.1.2	Condition number
output condition number
This command output the estimated condition number of matrix before solving the equations. Enable this command will significantly increase the running time. Use this only in testing, for example, when iteration fails.

1.8.2	Sample configuration
!> **********************************************************
!>              Block:  Output setting
!> **********************************************************

OUTPUT RUNTIME STATISTICS ANALYSIS

OUTPUT CONDITION NUMBER


***Updates***
1. Update the setting of Pardiso solver.
1. Update makefile, user mannual for parallel version of MIN3P and parallel configuration. 

***Bug fixes***
1. For some specific cases, Newton iteration fails. This bug is caused by level of accuracy, making some simulation fail in iteration.
1) adding "/fp:strict" to the compilation option, which has higher level of accuracy and result consistency for floating-point calculation.
2) adding explicit interface instead of external statement for the subroutine/functions with optional parameters.
2. 

***Notes***
1. Condition number estimation is time-consuming. Do not use this in the normal use. This is mainly used for matrix check when newton iteration failed. 
There will be two condition number exported: classical condition number and skeel's condition number. 
If both condition numbers are very large (e.g., > 1.0e12), the matrix are ill-conditioned and Pardiso may fail in solving it. 
WatSolv solver is more accurate in handling ill-conditioned matrix.
2. The code of estimating condition number is "Academic licence", read the LICENCE file for more information.
3. Input format is not changed when update the length of name from 12 to 72. 
For the free format database, it can support name length longer than 12. 
But for the fixed format database, it only read 12 characters. This should be modified later.
4. Installation of redistributable libraries.
Redistributable libraries are needed for the OS without developing environment. Currently, the 
program is compiled with Intel Visual Fortran compiler. Before running the parallel version of
MIN3P, please install Intel(R) Visual Redistributables on the system first. 
For 32-bit OS, please install Intel(R) Visual Fortran Redistributables on IA-32; For 64-bit OS,
please install Intel(R) Visual Fortran Redistributables on 64.
The Redistributable libraries corresponding to the developing environment can be downloaded via the URL
http://software.intel.com/en-us/articles/redistributable-libraries-for-intel-c-and-visual-
fortran-composer-xe-2013-for-windows.
5. Selection of executable files
There are two levels of executable file: normal level accuracy and high level accuracy. The
executalbe files of normal level accuracy are compiled with the default setting ("/fp:fast")
for floating-point calculation while the executable files of high level accuracy are compiled
with "/fp:strict" for floating-point calculation. For most of the cases, normal level accuracy
works fine. But for some special cases, high level accuracy executable files are needed,
otherwise, the solver maybe fails.

Message:
***Bug Fixes***
1. This bug is caused by level of accuracy, making some simulation fail in iteration.
1) adding "/fp:strict" to the compilation option, which has higher level of accuracy and result consistency for floating-point calculation.
2) adding explicit interface instead of external statement for the subroutine/functions with optional parameters. This is needed for win32-release mode, not necessary for win32-debug, x64-debug, x64-release. But to be safe and compatible to other compilers, explicit interface should always be used for the  subroutine/functions with optional parameters.

Revision: 75
Author: dsu
Date: April-29-13 9:59:56 PM
Message:
***Bug Fix***
1. Fix bug when 'pitzer model' is used. Currently, this bug is resolved by adding critical section for the pointer.

***Note***
For pointer data, multiple threads share the same address of the data, this will cause race condition problem. The best way is to avoid using pointer, use allocable array instead, which can have different copy for each thread and it is safe.

Revision: 69
Author: dsu
Date: April-18-13 10:25:32 AM
Message:
***Update***
1. New solver control parameters for paradiso solver.

***Usage***
Input example of solver.cfg

!*****Revision Informations Automatically Generated by VisualSVN*****!
!---------------------------------------------------------------------
!> $ID:$
!> $Revision: 33 $
!> $Author: dsu $
!> $Date: 2013-01-31 09:01:03 -0800 (Thu, 31 Jan 2013) $
!> $URL: https://biot.eos.ubc.ca/svn/min3p_thcm/branches/dsu_p/src/solver/solver.cfg $
!---------------------------------------------------------------------
!********************************************************************!

! **********************************************************
!              Block 1:  Golbal solver setting
! **********************************************************
! Select solver
! i_solver_type = 0 use ws209 solver
!                 1 use pardiso solver
! Note: this setting is for both flow solver and reactive transport solver
!SOLVER TYPE
!1

! i_solver_type_flow = 0 use ws209 solver
!                      1 use pardiso solver
SOLVER TYPE FLOW
1

! i_solver_type_react = 0 use ws209 solver
!                       1 use pardiso solver
SOLVER TYPE REACTIVE TRANSPORT
1

! i_symfactor_type_flow = 0 symbolic factorization at first run
!                         1 symbolic factorization every step
SYMBOLIC FACTORIZATION TYPE FLOW
1

! i_symfactor_type_react = 0 symbolic factorization at first run
!                          1 symbolic factorization every step
SYMBOLIC FACTORIZATION TYPE REACTIVE TRANSPORT
1


! **********************************************************
!              Block 2:  Pardiso solver setting
! **********************************************************

! Set this parameter if you want to check the result of matrix
! solver with ws209. Only valid if  i_solver_type = 0
! PARDISO: SOLVER TEST WITH WS209

! Set the number of threads for pardiso solver.
! If the solver type is not pardiso, ignore it.
! If the number of threads is less than 1, use dynamic
! number of threads determined by pardiso. 
PARDISO: NUMBER OF THREADS
0

! Maximum number of iterative refinement steps that the 
! solver will perform. The solver will perform not more 
! than the absolute value of this parameter for iterative
! refinement and will stop the process if a satisfactory
! level of accuracy of the solution in terms of backward
! error has been achieved.
! If this parameter is negative, the accumulation of the
! residuum is using extended precision real types.
! By default, the value is 9
PARDISO: MAX ITERATIVE REFINEMENT STEPS IN FLOW
9

PARDISO: MAX ITERATIVE REFINEMENT STEPS IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
9

! This parameter instructs PARDISO how to handle small
! pivots or zero pivots for unsymmetric matrices. 
! It indicates the iterative refinement contraction
! rate. The default value is 13, which means eps = 10^(-13)
! is used in handling small pivots.
PARDISO: PIVOTING PERTURBATION IN FLOW
13

PARDISO: PIVOTING PERTURBATION IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
13

! This parameter controls preconditioned CGS [Sonn89] for nonsymmetric matrices. iparm(4) has the form iparm(4)= 10*L+K.
! K=0 The factorization is always computed as required by phase.
! K=1 CGS iteration replaces the computation of LU. The preconditioner is LU that was computed at a previous step 
! (the first step or last step with a failure) in a sequence of solutions needed for identical sparsity patterns.
! iparm(4)    Description 
! 31    LU-preconditioned CGS iteration with a stopping criterion of 1.0E-3 for nonsymmetric matrices 
! 61    LU-preconditioned CGS iteration with a stopping criterion of 1.0E-6 for nonsymmetric matrices 
PARDISO: CGS CRITERION IN FLOW
61

PARDISO: CGS CRITERION IN REACTIVE TRANSPORT
61

! **********************************************************
!              Block 3:  Output setting
! **********************************************************

! Set if output detail runtime statistics analysis.
! Comment out if you do not want to export it.
OUTPUT RUNTIME STATISTICS ANALYSIS

Revision: 67
Author: dsu
Date: April-15-13 12:08:40 AM
Message:
Summary of branch: Revision 59 to 66

xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx
xxx Summary of branch: Revision 59 to 66
xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

***New features***
1.Add maximum and minimum threshold for permeability scaling factor. For the previous version, the permeability scaling factor can range from 0 to a huge number, which may cause unrealistic result in matrix solver.

2. Add threshold "porosity threshold maximum" and "porosity threshold minimum" when "update porosity" is used. This is to avoid division by 0 in Kozeny-Carman equation and to avoid porosity 0. 

3. Add 'twothird-mix' type in calculating surface area. 'twothird-mix' is based on 'two-third', when the phases have the initial volume fraction (phi_o) < the threshold value (phinuc), the initial surface area (scalfac) is used as the surface area, otherwise, 'two-third' method is used.

4. Update algorithm of residual to norm for pardiso solver. Three algorithms are considered: (a) The L^1 vector norm, (b) the L^2 (Euclidean) vector norm, and (3) the L^infinity vector norm. Current L^2 (Euclidean) vector norm is used in Pardiso solver.

***Usage of new features***

1. Usage of feature 1: 
  Add the following commands to Data Block 7
  'permeability scaling factor threshold maximum'
  1.0d10
  'permeability scaling factor threshold minimum'
  1.0d-10

2. Usage of feature 2: 
  Add the following commands to Data Block 7:
  'update porosity'
  'porosity threshold maximum'
  0.9999d0
  'porosity threshold minimum'
  0.0001d0

3. Usage of feature 3:
  !Example in input file:
  0.00d0   .false. 'twothird-mix'  ;phim, minequil, update_type  - 'calcite'       
  1.d-10    1.0d-1    0.00d0    1.0d-7    ;phimin, scalfac, supsatm, phinuc


***Modification***


***Bug fixes***


***Notes***
In revision 60, feature 2: The hydraulic conductivity exported to the tecplot is following the formula : "condzz(nzn)*perm_fac(ivol)/sec_per_days. For the previous version, the exported hydraulic conductivity is not strict the same as that used in the internal calculation. When porosity is 0 or 1, perm_fac will not updated to avoid division by 0 in Kozeny-Carman equation.This was fixed in revision 60.

In revision 59, feature 3: This is an alternative method to apply 'twothird' and 'constant' for the same minerals to different zones. At present, update-type is mineral-dependent, say, the same mineral has the same update-type (e.g, 'constant') for different zones. For example, if we have the following zone 1 and zone 2. In zone 1, the initial volume fraction is 0 < 1.0d-7, then the initial surface 1.0d-1 is used. In zone 2, the initial volume fraction 0.25 > 1.0d-7, the surface area is calculated based on 'two-third' method.

zone 1
0.00d0   .false. 'twothird-mix'  ;phim, minequil, update_type  - 'calcite'       
1.d-10    1.0d-1    0.00d0    1.0d-7    ;phimin, scalfac, supsatm, phinuc

zone 2
0.25d0   .false. 'twothird-mix'  ;phim, minequil, update_type  - 'calcite'       
1.d-10    1.0d-1    0.00d0    1.0d-7    ;phimin, scalfac, supsatm, phinuc

Revision: 59
Author: dsu
Date: March-14-13 3:41:21 PM
Message:
***New features:
1. Add 'twothird-mix' type in calculating surface area. 'twothird-mix' is based on 'two-third', when the  phases have the initial volume fraction (phi_o) < the threshold value (phinuc), the initial surface area (scalfac) is used as the surface area, otherwise, 'two-third' method is used.

***Example in input file:

0.00d0   .false. 'twothird-mix'  ;phim, minequil, update_type  - 'calcite'       
1.d-10    1.0d-1    0.00d0    1.0d-7    ;phimin, scalfac, supsatm, phinuc

***Note: 
This is an alternative method to apply 'twothird' and 'constant' for the same minerals to different zones. At present, update-type is mineral-dependent, say, the same mineral has the same update-type (e.g, 'constant') for different zones. For example, if we have the following zone 1 and zone 2. In zone 1, the initial volume fraction is 0 < 1.0d-7, then the initial surface 1.0d-1 is used. In zone 2, the initial volume fraction 0.25 > 1.0d-7, the surface area is calculated based on 'two-third' method.

zone 1
0.00d0   .false. 'twothird-mix'  ;phim, minequil, update_type  - 'calcite'       
1.d-10    1.0d-1    0.00d0    1.0d-7    ;phimin, scalfac, supsatm, phinuc

zone 2
0.25d0   .false. 'twothird-mix'  ;phim, minequil, update_type  - 'calcite'       
1.d-10    1.0d-1    0.00d0    1.0d-7    ;phimin, scalfac, supsatm, phinuc

Revision: 63
Author: mxie
Date: April-08-13 5:05:50 PM
Message:
***New features***

1. Bug fix: Total sorbed mass calculation: previous version valid only for one material zone. Improvement is extension and valid for different zones or distributed case.

2. Added Harmonic averaging of effective diffusion coefficient for radial and 1D 2D & 3D cartesian coordinate.

3. Added verification benchmarks


Usage of the 2. extension:

A. arithmetic averaging of effective diffusion coefficient (De). De
     averaging is undertaken in the way: 
	1. calculate por_av=[por(ivol)+por(jvol)]/2,
	2. calculate De_av=tau_av*por_av*sat_av*D0

 by adding the following keywords and subkeywords in "! Data Block 7: control parameters - reactive transport":

  'averaging diffusion'         ; Keyword
    'arithmetic'                ; De_av = D0*por_av*tau_av


B. arithmetic averaging of effective diffusion coefficient (De). De
     averaging is undertaken in the way: 
	1. calculate calculate De(ivol) and De(jvol),
	2. calculate De_av=[De(ivol)+De(jvol)]/2

 by adding the following keywords and subkeywords in "! Data Block 7: control parameters - reactive transport":

  'averaging diffusion'         ; Keyword
    'arithmetic De'              ; De_av=[De(i)+De(j)]/2, in which De(i)=D0*por_i*tau_i

C. harmonic averaging of effective diffusion coefficient (De). De
     averaging is undertaken in the way:
	1. calculate calculate De(ivol) and De(jvol),
	2. calculate De_av=[De(ivol)*De(jvol)*(delx_i+delx_j)]/[De(ivol)*delx_i+De(jvol)*delx_j]

 by adding the following keywords and subkeywords in "! Data Block 7: control parameters - reactive transport":

  'averaging diffusion'         ; Keyword
  'harmonic'


Note: 1. It is recommended to use the averaging methods for porosity and diffusion coefficeint in a consistant way. That is to say, if harmonic averaging of De is desired, the porosity should be averaged also harmonically by just adding 'harmonic average in porosity' in the Data Block 16: boundary conditions - reactive transport.
      2. if the keyword 'averaging diffusion' is not provided in the input file, the default method A is used.

----

Author: dsu

Message:
Commit after reintegrating the branch (Revision 39 to 55)

xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx
xxx Summary of branch: Revision 39 to 55
xxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx

***New features:
1. Revision 41: Add biomass growth codes to MIN3P_THCm.
2. Revision 46 to 49: Update reactive surface area for linear relationship with mass volume fraction. The reference surface area is calculated by S(ref) = S(phi_max) * phi_nuc / phi_max, where phi_max is the given bulk volume fraction and S(phi_max) is the given surface area under the phi_max.
3. Revision 50: Add new input parameter 'consider exponent n for reaction rate law'. 
4. Revision 51: Write version informations to the output files. 
5. Revision 52: Add porosity threshold to avoid division by 0 in Kozeny-Carman equation.
6. Revision 53: Add tortuosity update. Current tortuosity is updated as Tau = tau_0 por^alpha/por_o^alpha or Tau = por^alpha
7. Assign tortuosity factor (alpha, archie) to different zones. Previous codes apply the same factor to the whole domain when tortuosity correction is set to 'archie'.


***Modification:
1. Revision 42: Modification in calculating prodrc (activity product of species involved in reaction) and prodrcinc (activity product of species involved in reaction (incremented)). For an irreversible dissolution reaction, the reaction should be excluded, if (1-IAP/K) < 0, no matter what prodrcinc is. For an irreversible precipitation reaction, the reaction should be excluded, if (1-IAP/K) >  0, no matter what prodrcinc is.

2. Revision 44 and 45: Add the routines for switching the (1-IAP/K) term (alternative formulation). 


***Bug fixes:
1. Revision 43: Fix bug in calculating ratemp in modrate.F90.
    if (dabs(ratem).lt.tinyrate) then
      ratemp(ireac) = r0
    end if
    ...
    ratemp(ireac) = ratemp(ireac) * ratem_mod/ratem

The default tinyrate value is 0 in the codes, without the command line 'define minimum reaction rate', the above codes will cause NaN in ratemp when ratem equals 0.
Change the default value of tinyrate from 0 to 1.0D-300 to avoid division by 0.

2. Revision 53: Fix bug in calculating diffusion coefficient use 'archie' in diffcoff.F90. 
previous codes:
            case('archie')
              diffcoff = diff_p * sat_p * por**m_archie 
current codes:
            case('archie')
              diffcoff = diff_p * sat_p * por**(m_archie + 1) 

3. Revision 53: Fix bug in calculating satav in infcrtdd.F90
previous codes: 
            satav=dmin1(r1, sanew(ivol))	!ivol is not corrent, should use jvol
current codes:
            satav=dmin1(r1, sanew(jvol))  


***Notes:
1. Please note that exponent n is by default considered for revision 42-44 and 49. If exponent n is considered, you will find "consider exponent n for reaction rate law: true" in the log file, otherwise, you will find "consider exponent n for reaction rate law: false". If exponent n is not considered but the supplied exponent n is not 1, then you will find warning information "WARNING: supplied exponent n will be ingnored and is set to 1" in the log file.


***Usage of new features:
1. Usage of feature 1: 

Add 'biomass components' to data block 2.

Examples:
'components'
12                            ;number of components 
'h+1'                         ;component names
'na+1'                     
'ca+2'
'cl-1'
'co3-2'
'ch3coo-'
'no3-1'
'no2-1'
'n2(aq)'
'nh4+1'
'cro4-2'
'cr(oh)2+'

'biomass components'          
2                             ;number of biomass components
'c5h7o2n'                     ;biomass component names
'c5h7o2n(d)'

2. Usage of feature 2:

set update type of mineral to 'linear' and define the value of nucleation threshold, e.g., 1.0E-7.

Examples:

  'mineral input'   
  0.018879  .false. 'linear'  ;phim, minequil, update_type  - c3fh6
  1.d-10    1.0d-9    0.00d0    1.0d-7    ;phimin, scalfac, supsatm, phic_nuc
  0.127803  .false. 'linear'  ;phim, minequil, update_type  - csh(1.6)
  1.d-10    1.0d-9    0.00d0    1.0d-7    ;phimin, area, supsatm, phic_nuc

3. Usage of feature 3:
This parameter is read from the input file. Default setting does not consider exponent n for reaction rate law. Add the command 'consider exponent n for reaction rate law' to 'Data Block 2: geochemical system' if exponent n is required in reaction rate law.

4. Usage of feature 4:
By default, the program only write version informations to the log file and gen file. If you want to write version information to the tecplot data file, please add the command 'write version to tecplot data file' to "Data Block 1: global control parameters".

5. Usage of feature 5:
Add 'porosity threshold' to Data Block 7:

Examples: 

  'porosity threshold'
  0.99d0

If porosity threshold is not set in the input file, then the default value 1.0d0 will be used.

6. Usage of feature 6 and feature 7:

Add 'update tortuosity' to block 7 and alpha value to block 9.

Examples in block 7:

'tortuosity correction'
'assigned tau' 
'update tortuosity'

Examples in block 9:

'number and name of zone'
1
'concrete'
0.1       ;porosity
0.0383    ;tortuosity
2.0       ;alpha, tortuosity update factor tau=tau_0*(por/por_0)^alpha

Revision: 53
Author: dsu
Date: March-01-13 3:02:08 PM
Message:
***New features:
1. Add tortuosity update. 
Current tortuosity is updated as follows:
Method 1: Tau = tau_0 por^alpha/por_o^alpha. 
Method 2: Tau = por^alpha
2. Assign tortuosity factor (alpha, archie) to different zones. Previous codes apply the same factor to the whole domain when tortuosity correction is set to 'archie'. After modifying, you shall set this value for different zone in block 9. 
The benchmarking scenarios do not include 'archie' tortuosity correction, so the modified codes can work with the previous benchmarking.

***How to use:

Add 'update tortuosity' to block 7 and alpha value to block 9.

e.g., in block 7:
'tortuosity correction'
'assigned tau' 
'update tortuosity'

in block 9:

'number and name of zone'
1
'concrete'
0.1       ;porosity
0.0383    ;tortuosity
2.0       ;alpha, tortuosity update factor tau=tau_0*(por/por_0)^alpha

***Bug fix:
previous codes in diffcoff.F90
-----------------------
            case('archie')
              diffcoff = diff_p * sat_p * por**m_archie 
------------------------      
            
Dp = Do x tau
Deff = Dp * por
Deff = Do x tau x por
Deff = Do x por^m_archie x por = Do x por^(m_archie +1)

so the correct equation should be
-----------------------
            case('archie')
              diffcoff = diff_p * sat_p * por**(m_archie + 1) 
------------------------ 

***Further check:
1. Theoretically, when tau_0 = por_0^alpha, method 1 and method 2 shall generate the same result, but in the test (decalcification-of-concrete), the results are slight different. This may due to average in tortuosity and alpha (archie) factor in infcrt_a.F90, infcrt_g.F90 and et. al. Further check is needed on this problem.
----

Revision: 50

Date: February-19-13 10:32:05 AM
Message:
Updates:
1. The parameter of 'consider exponent n for reaction rate law' is now read from the input file. Default setting does not consider exponent n for reaction rate law. Please add the command 'consider exponent n for reaction rate law' to 'Data Block 2: geochemical system' if exponent n is required in reaction rate law.

Notes:
1. Please note that exponent n is by default considered for revision 42-44 and 49.
2. If exponent n is considered, you will find "consider exponent n for reaction rate law: true" in the log file, otherwise, you will find "consider exponent n for reaction rate law: false". If exponent n is not considered but the supplied exponent n is not 1, then you will find warning information "WARNING: supplied exponent n will be ingnored and is set to 1" in the log file.

Revision: 49
Author: dsu
Date: February-19-13 9:02:44 AM
Message:
Updates:
1. Major update for reference surface area and nucleation threshold. The reference surface area is calculated by S(ref) = S(phi_max) * phi_nuc / phi_max, where phi_max is the given bulk volume fraction and S(phi_max) is the given surface area under the phi_max.

Revision: 46
Author: dsu
Date: February-17-13 11:22:08 PM
Message:
New feature:
1. Update reactive surface area for linear relationship with mass volume fraction.

This part is initially for the simulation of concrete-clay benchmarking. In PHREEQC, they use R = S*m*k to update the rate, here we use R = S’ * phi/phi_o, where phi_o is the volume fraction. This won’t work if phi_0 = 0, or if phi becomes 0. We define a mineral mass nucleation threshold and calculate a reference surface area S’ for this nucleation threshold. This is different from current formulation. The current formulation defines the reference surface area based on the initial mineral mass. Below the nucleation threshold, we can use this reference surface area to define a constant S’, above the surface area is larger, linearly scaled with phi/phi_nuc: 

How to use: set update type to 'linear' and define the value of nucleation threshold, e.g., 1.0E-7.

Sample data in input file:

  'mineral input'   
  0.018879  .false. 'linear'  ;phim, minequil, update_type  - c3fh6
  1.d-10    1.0d-9    0.00d0    1.0d-7    ;phimin, scalfac, supsatm, phic_nuc
  0.127803  .false. 'linear'  ;phim, minequil, update_type  - csh(1.6)
  1.d-10    1.0d-9    0.00d0    1.0d-7    ;phimin, area, supsatm, phic_nuc
----

Revision: 43
Author: dsu
Date: February-12-13 10:27:58 AM
Message:
Fix bug in modrate.F90

    if (dabs(ratem).lt.tinyrate) then
      ratemp(ireac) = r0
    end if
    ...
    ratemp(ireac) = ratemp(ireac) * ratem_mod/ratem

The default tinyrate value is 0 in the codes, without the command line 'define minimum reaction rate', the above codes will cause NaN in ratemp when ratem equals 0.
I suggest to change the default value of tinyrate from 0 to 1.0E-300 in the codes and replace the computing of ratemp as follows:

    if (dabs(ratem).lt.tinyrate) then
      ratemp(ireac) = r0
      continue
    end if
    ...
    ratemp(ireac) = ratemp(ireac) * ratem_mod/ratem

or

    if (dabs(ratem).lt.tinyrate) then
      ratemp(ireac) = r0
    end if
    ...
    if (dabs(ratem) < tinyrate) then
        ratemp(ireac) = r0
    else
        ratemp(ireac) = ratemp(ireac) * ratem_mod/ratem
    end if

The former one is used in the revised codes.
----
Modified : /branches/dsu2/src/min3p/initcplc.F90
Modified : /branches/dsu2/src/min3p/modrate.F90

Revision: 42
Author: dsu
Date: February-08-13 12:08:49 PM
Message:
Modification in calculating prodrc (activity product of species involved in reaction) and prodrcinc (activity product of species involved in reaction (incremented)).
For an irreversible dissolution reaction, the reaction should be excluded, if (1-IAP/K) < 0, no matter what prodrcinc is.
For an irreversible precipitation reaction, the reaction should be excluded, if (1-IAP/K) >  0, no matter what prodrcinc is.

Revision: 37
Author: dsu
Date: January-31-13 5:12:03 PM
Message:
Summary of this branch

New features added in this branch:
1. Add runtime statistics analysis of jacobi matrix construction, factorization and substitution for different equations.
2. Add Pardiso parallel solver (factorization and substitution) and solver configuration. Further check is needed to correct the error and improve the parallel efficiency. Not recommend to use at present.
3. Modify read statement for complex and gases so as to support more than 5 components.
4. Modify the formula in calculating prodrc and prodrcinc by taking an absolute value term and switch signs. The previous formula may cause NaN problem when n is not equal 1.

Miscellaneous:
1. New tool added: mtxView.exe. This tool is used for sparse matrix visualization. Input data should be matrix market exchange formats (zlib compress of data file is acceptable).
2. Modify input file read, add the function getarg to read in arguments, making the program able to start from command line. Command format: min3p.exe inputfile.
3. Modify syntax warning in fortran output format. The output format like '(a,i4,a,e10.3)/' is not correct, should use '(a,i4,a,e10.3,/)' instead.
4. Add version stamp (revision, build time) to the start logo of the program.
5. Add project folder visualstudio_p for parallel development environment. It shares the same source codes with serial development environment.

New features merged from trunk:
1. Integration of the multicomponent diffusion function developed by Pejman into the MIN3P-THCm main version.
2. Control volume calculation for radial coordinate mesh.

----

Message:
Unversion and ignore the code file Version.F90. This file is automatically generated during pre-build event based on the template VersionTemplate.F90. Everytime you rebuild your project, the Version information will be changed. This would cause conflicts between different user so that I unversion it.
Please note: the version information in Version.F90 is generated by SubWCRev.exe, which is integrated into TortoiseSVN program.

2. Add version control informations and embed version information into source codes.

1. Add cross-platform configuration into the project using preprocessor. The project can be compiled under Windows and CYGWIN.
